== #design-and-eda: 317 lines before, 320 after; as printed only before 35, only after 38; marks out only before 1, only after 4; card 49 lines, the same words; order with the card set aside the same
   sources: 60 lines before, 60 after, the same set, in the new reading order
   the card before: after "A GPU die based on Nvidia's published H100 block diagram. It is drawn ", before "In plain terms"
   the card after:  after "Design is the only stage of the supply chain that happens entirely in ", before "How it works#"
   words only before: Extreme
   words only after:  Extreme concentration
   words only after:  ·
   words only after:  Hard to substitute
   words only after:  Chokepoint card
   - Extreme
   - Only three complete sets of chip design software exist1. A fourth would have to be written from nothing and approved by a foundry2.
   - world market for design software, simulation software and licensed circuit blocks in 20252. SemiAnalysis put a single respin, a design revision that needs some new masks, at leading-edge nodes at $50-100M in May 20262
   - 90%+ of the timing-signoff market at advanced nodes as of 20262
   - 55-60% of the hardware emulator market at advanced nodes as of 20262
   - 85%+ of physical verification, the final layout check, at advanced nodes as of 20262
   - About 50% of processor compute at the top cloud firms as of May 20263
   - $16.7B of AI semiconductor revenue in the third quarter of fiscal 2026, custom accelerators and networking combined4
   - Changing one tool, such as the synthesis tool that turns a design's code into logic cells, means re-running the later steps of the design2. The foundry that makes the chip also specifies which signoff tools its customers must use2.
   - China designs its own AI accelerators, but Huawei's most powerful chip in 2026, the Ascend 950, delivers about half the computing performance of Nvidia's H100 from 2022, on Epoch AI's estimate5. CSIS found that as of 2025 Chinese design tools were still far from replacing the full range of advanced capabilities US firms offer, especially for 5 nm and 3 nm chips6.
   - Synopsys and Cadence, the two largest design-software vendors by 2025 revenue, are American2.
   - Chip design turns an idea for a new chip into a complete blueprint. A chip holds billions of transistors, tiny electrical switches. The blueprint fixes where each one sits and how it is wired to the others. The chip runs on a clock, and a signal has to cross those wires within one tick, less than a billionth of a second. Software from three companies, Synopsys, Cadence and Siemens, lays out the blueprint, checks it and signs it off. A foundry, a factory that makes chips for other companies, accepts only designs that have passed that software's checks. The foundry prints each layer of the design onto silicon through a stencil called a mask. Changing the blueprint once the masks are made takes new masks and a few months7.
   - The US government required a license to sell design software to anyone in China from late May to 2 July 20258. Since October 2022 a narrower requirement has covered software specially made for chips with gate-all-around transistors, a transistor design the rule calls key to processes at 3 nm and below9.
   - As of 2025, the think tank CSIS found Chinese design tools still far from replacing the full range of advanced capabilities US firms offer, especially for 5 nm and 3 nm chip design6. But Chinese design-software companies were winning share at older manufacturing processes, the research firm SemiAnalysis reported2.
   - Design is the only stage of the supply chain that happens entirely in software. The US government has restricted it with letters to the companies that sell that software. In late May 2025 the government required a license to sell chip design software to anyone in China8. Cadence cut off its Chinese customers until the government lifted the requirement on 2 July8.
   - All that checking exists because each revision after tape-out, called a respin, needs at least some new masks7. In 2022 SemiAnalysis put a full mask set at more than $1 million at 28 nm and more than $10 million at 7 nm7. The nanometer labels name process generations, called nodes, each finer than the last, and no longer measure anything. In 2022 SemiAnalysis also expected 3 nm sets to push into the $40 million range7. Its May 2026 primer still puts a leading-edge mask set at $40 million, and a single leading-edge respin at $50 million to $100 million2.
   - The same 2022 SemiAnalysis article reported that startups had taken chips on TSMC 7 nm, then a leading-edge process, from design to tape-out for $50 million to $75 million, everything included7.
   - Source SemiAnalysis, The Dark Side of the Semiconductor Design Renaissance, July 2022. The 3 nm figure was a 2022 forecast. SemiAnalysis's May 2026 EDA Market Primer puts a leading-edge mask set at $40M 7
   - Chip design software is known as electronic design automation, or EDA. Three complete EDA flows exist, each a set of tools that covers every step from code to signoff1. SemiAnalysis wrote in May 2026 that no startup can replicate one by writing it from scratch2. Changing the synthesis tool, which swaps the code for logic cells, means re-running the steps that follow: placing and wiring the cells, signoff and physical verification2. Foundries also specify which signoff tools their customers must use for tape-out2.
   - For chips at advanced nodes, 7 nm and below, one vendor holds most of the market inside some steps of the flow. Synopsys alone has more than 90 percent of the market for static timing analysis, the check that every signal arrives in time at the chip's rated speed2. In May 2026 SemiAnalysis put Siemens EDA's Calibre at more than 85 percent of physical verification, the final layout check2. It put Cadence's Palladium at 55 to 60 percent of hardware emulation2.
   - Source SemiAnalysis, EDA Market Primer, May 2026, table of tool-level shares at advanced nodes 2
   - Arm licenses processor designs. In fiscal 2026 it reported $4.92 billion of revenue, $2.61 billion of it royalties3. It said Arm-based designs held about 50 percent of the computing power of central processors (CPUs) at the top cloud firms3.
   - In 2026 Arm also launched the Arm AGI CPU, the first finished chip that Arm sells3. By May 2026, servers built on it could be ordered from ASRock, Lenovo, Quanta and Supermicro3.
   - Design software, simulation software and licensed blocks made up an $18 billion market in 2025, on SemiAnalysis's count2. Synopsys, Cadence and Siemens EDA took more than 85 percent of it, counting Ansys as part of Synopsys2. No other vendor had more than 5 percent of any of the main categories of design software2. Siemens EDA is a US-based subsidiary of the German firm Siemens16.
   - The two fiscal years do not line up, so any total for this market depends on which twelve months are counted. It also depends on how much of Ansys the total includes, because Ansys sells to other industries too. SemiAnalysis puts 31 percent of Ansys's business in semiconductors and high technology, 22 percent in aerospace and 18 percent in automotive2. It counts Synopsys at $8 billion for calendar 2025 with Ansys included2. On the same count Cadence is second, at $5.30 billion, and Siemens EDA third, at an estimated $2.2 billion to $2.5 billion2.
   - Source SemiAnalysis, EDA Market Primer, May 2026, table of calendar 2025 vendor shares 2
   - Chip firms such as Broadcom build custom accelerators to a customer's design and sell the finished chips. Broadcom booked $16.7 billion of AI semiconductor revenue in the third quarter of fiscal 2026, up 221 percent year over year4. That figure covers both its custom AI accelerators and its AI networking chips4.
   - Before the 2025 letters, a narrower control already applied. Since 14 October 2022, design software specially made for chips with gate-all-around transistors has needed a US export license for national-security reasons9. The rule calls that transistor design key to processes at 3 nm and below9. That license requirement covers exports to China24. The control carried out a December 2021 decision of the Wassenaar Arrangement, a group of countries that coordinate export controls9.
   - In late May 2025 the US Bureau of Industry and Security, the agency that issues export licenses, told the design-software vendors that sales to China now needed a license8. Cadence got its letter on 23 May8. The letter required a license to export design software and technology classified 3D991 and 3E991 to any party in China, or to a Chinese military end user anywhere8. Synopsys got its letter on 29 May and a second letter lifting the requirement on 2 July25. Unlike the 2022 control, the 2025 requirement came from the United States alone and took effect immediately.
   - Synopsys's China revenue fell from 16 percent of its total in fiscal 2024 to 12 percent in fiscal 20252. SemiAnalysis links the decline to tighter export restrictions and to local design-software companies winning share at mature nodes2. SemiAnalysis also quoted Synopsys's management saying that when Synopsys cannot sell to a company, that company looks for alternatives, typically local design-software and licensed-block companies2.
   - In October 2025 CSIS found that only three companies, Cadence, Synopsys and Siemens, make design software that supports the entire chip design flow of more than 40 steps1. In a January 2021 report built on 2019 market data, the Center for Security and Emerging Technology (CSET) at Georgetown University found that one Chinese firm, Empyrean, could reportedly run a complete design flow only for some analog and mixed-signal chips26. Mixed-signal chips combine analog and digital circuits. For all other chips, Chinese designers then relied on US tools26. The report found that other Chinese tools covered only narrow parts of the design flow or supplemented US tools, and largely did not support processes at 14 nm and below26.
   - Empyrean says it has covered more of the design flow since then. Its 2025 annual report, filed in April 2026, says it now has complete design flows for analog, memory, radio-frequency and flat-panel display circuits27. It says it offers 80 percent of the main tools used in digital design28. The same report lists foundry certifications for its circuit simulator at 8, 5 and 4 nm and for its physical verification tool, the final layout check, at 28 nm29. But CSIS found that as of 2025 Chinese design tools were still far from replacing the full range of advanced capabilities US firms offer, especially for 5 nm and 3 nm chip design6.
   - China can design accelerators, but it cannot finish one at the leading edge without foreign tools, or build what it designs without foreign equipment. SMIC, China's leading foundry, makes chips for Huawei5. In June 2024 CSET found that SMIC's limited 7 nm capacity forced Huawei to choose between making its most advanced phone chips and its AI chips30.
   - Epoch AI estimates that Huawei trails Nvidia both in what each chip can do and in how many chips it makes5. On Epoch's estimates, Huawei's flagship chip for 2026, the Ascend 950, delivers about half the computing performance of Nvidia's H100, which began shipping in 20225. Epoch puts Nvidia's B300 at 6.75 times the 950's theoretical peak performance5. Epoch's median estimate is that Huawei will make about 1.5 million Ascend chips in 2026, against 5.9 million for Nvidia5. On those estimates, Huawei's 2026 output would carry less than 4 percent of the computing power Nvidia ships that year5.
   - Epoch puts Huawei's chips three to four years behind Nvidia's until at least 2030, for three reasons5. SMIC cannot pack transistors as densely as TSMC, which makes Nvidia's. Chinese memory makers are only starting to produce high-bandwidth memory. And Huawei's software for programming its chips is far less mature than Nvidia's5.

== #silicon-and-wafers: 286 lines before, 289 after; as printed only before 38, only after 41; marks out only before 1, only after 4; card 49 lines, the same words; order with the card set aside the same
   sources: 60 lines before, 60 after, the same set, in the new reading order
   the card before: after "A silicon ingot, one crystal pulled whole from a melt, with two wafers", before "In plain terms"
   the card after:  after "The world market for silicon wafers was $11.4 billion in 2025, on 12,9", before "How it works#"
   words only before: High
   words only after:  High concentration
   words only after:  ·
   words only after:  Moderately hard to substitute
   words only after:  Chokepoint card
   - High
   - Several firms already make 300 mm wafers that fabs have approved, but customers have to test and approve a new wafer plant before they buy from it1.
   - world wafer market in 2025, on 12,973 million square inches shipped2
   - Its parent's electronics materials segment, wafers included, had ¥1,015.7bn of sales in the year to March 20263
   - ¥409,670m of net sales in 20254
   - NT$60.6bn revenue in 20255
   - One of the top five wafer makers by revenue in the first three quarters of 20256
   - €1,346.7m revenue in 20257
   - Losing one of the top five wafer makers leaves four, and a new wafer plant can still be built6. Customer approval of a new plant is called qualification1. Siltronic completed key customer qualifications at its new Singapore fab in July 20251.
   - China made about 93 percent of the world's polysilicon in 2023, and an estimated 98 percent of China's output was solar-grade8. Eswin, mainland China's largest maker of 300 mm wafers, says it had about 7.7 percent of world 300 mm shipments in the first half of 20269.
   - None of the five largest wafer makers in 2025 was American6. GlobalWafers of Taiwan won CHIPS awards of up to $406 million in December 2024 for wafer plants in Texas and Missouri10.
   - Every chip is built on a wafer, a thin round slice of silicon about 30 centimeters across that carries hundreds of chips at once. Making one starts with quartz rock, which is silicon bound to oxygen11. The silicon is refined until metal impurities are no more than a few parts per billion12. It is then melted, and a small seed crystal is lowered to the surface and drawn slowly back up. The melt freezes onto the seed as one long cylinder, a single crystal with its atoms lined up in one unbroken pattern from end to end. Saws cut the cylinder into discs, and each disc is polished to a mirror finish and made extremely flat across its whole face12. In 2025 the top five wafer makers served around 75 percent of the world market, and none of them is American or Chinese6.
   - Japanese companies held 53 percent of the world's silicon wafer sales in 2023, by a Fuji Keizai survey that Japan's trade ministry reproduced13. Firms based in Taiwan, South Korea and Europe held most of the rest13. China's largest maker of 300 mm wafers, Eswin, says it ranked sixth in the world with about 7.7 percent of global 300 mm shipments in the first half of 20269. Chips also need electronic-grade polysilicon, the raw material for wafers14. The German wafer maker Siltronic named five major suppliers of it for 2025, and none is Chinese14.
   - GlobalWafers of Taiwan opened a $3.5 billion plant in Texas in May 2025, the first fully integrated plant for advanced silicon wafers built in the United States in over twenty years15. US Presidential Proclamation 11052 sets a minimum import price of $100 per kilogram for polysilicon ingots and wafers from 4 December 202616.
   - The world market for silicon wafers was $11.4 billion in 2025, on 12,973 million square inches shipped2. That is a small fraction of Nvidia's data center revenue, which reached $193.7 billion in the fiscal year to 25 January 202617.
   - In the next step, the Siemens process, the clean gas flows over silicon filaments heated inside a bell jar. On the hot surface the gas breaks apart and its silicon settles onto the filaments. Over days the filaments thicken into gray rods of polysilicon. Everything downstream is made from those rods. Polysilicon comes in two ultra-high-purity grades, one for semiconductors and one for solar cells, and chips use the semiconductor grade11.
   - A grower melts the polysilicon at about 1,420 degrees Celsius, hotter than lava, and pulls a single crystal out of it by the Czochralski method12. The grower lowers a small seed crystal on the end of a rod to the surface of the melt and draws it back up, slowly turning. Silicon freezes onto the seed in the seed's own atomic pattern, and a cylinder, the ingot, grows beneath it. The pull starts with a thin neck so that flaws from the seed's first contact with the melt stay in the neck and never reach the ingot. Boron or phosphorus is stirred into the melt, a step called doping. The added element sets how readily the wafer conducts and whether negative or positive charges carry its current.
   - Epitaxial wafers get one more layer of near-perfect silicon, grown on top at about 1,200 degrees Celsius12. That is still hot enough to glow orange.
   - A polished wafer is a doped single crystal polished to a mirror finish12. An epitaxial wafer is a polished wafer with a thin layer of single-crystal silicon grown on top12. The transistors are built in that layer, so flaws in it can make them leak current. SUMCO says an epitaxial wafer suits customers who need a near-perfect crystal or layers that differ in electrical resistance12. SEMI, the industry association, credits the 2025 rise in shipments to AI-driven demand for advanced epitaxial wafers for logic chips and polished wafers for high-bandwidth memory2.
   - In a silicon-on-insulator (SOI) wafer, a buried layer of oxide, which is glass, separates a thin working layer of silicon from the bulk beneath. Soitec says nearly all SOI wafers sold worldwide are made with its Smart Cut process18. Soitec's SOI product lines include RF-SOI for radio chips and fully depleted SOI (FD-SOI)18. Its markets run from mobile communications to cloud AI18. But for logic chips, SEMI credited the AI-driven growth in 2025 to a different kind of wafer, the advanced epitaxial wafer2.
   - Source METI, December 2025, from Fuji Keizai data (2023); Japan's and Europe's shares are printed, the others are read off METI's chart 13
   - Japanese companies held 53 percent of the world's silicon wafer sales in 202313. The chart shows shares of 2023 sales from a survey by Fuji Keizai, a Japanese research firm13. Japan's Ministry of Economy, Trade and Industry reproduced the figures in a December 2025 strategy deck13. The ministry's chart of the Fuji Keizai survey prints only Japan's 53 percent, so the other shares in the chart above are measured from its bars13. An earlier page of the ministry's deck prints Europe's share in the same Fuji Keizai survey as 10 percent22. The chart gives no separate share to Chinese or US companies13.
   - Siltronic's April 2026 investor presentation says the top five wafer makers serve around 75 percent of the market, based on revenue in the first three quarters of 20256. It names the five as Shin-Etsu, SUMCO, GlobalWafers, SK Siltron and Siltronic itself6. Siltronic describes itself as the only Western-based maker among the five6.
   - Shin-Etsu. Shin-Etsu Chemical's electronics materials segment, which includes its wafer business, sold ¥1,015.7 billion in the year to March 2026, up 9 percent3. AI-related demand stayed strong and demand in other sectors began to rise, lifting sales of silicon wafers, photoresist and photomask blanks3. The segment also sells rare earth magnets and other materials, and the results release does not break out wafer sales23. So the figure cannot be compared with the wafer revenue of the other firms listed.
   - SUMCO. Sold ¥409,670 million24. Its operating profit fell from ¥36,924 million to ¥1,342 million, and it ended the year with a net loss of ¥11,751 million24. In the same year it added leading-edge 300 mm capacity and reorganized production of 200 mm and smaller wafers, where demand stayed weak4.
   - Siltronic. Lost €77.9 million on €1,346.7 million of sales while investing €369.1 million, with its new Singapore fab as the focus7.
   - GlobalWafers. NT$60.6 billion of revenue, down 3.24 percent in local currency5.
   - Worldwide, the wafer area shipped rose 5.8 percent in 2025, but revenue fell 1.2 percent2. SEMI attributes the softer revenue mostly to traditional chip uses outside AI, where demand and pricing had yet to improve2.
   - Polysilicon is the raw material for both wafers and solar cells, and almost all of China's is made for solar cells. In 2023 China made 1.50 million metric tons of polysilicon, about 93 percent of world output8. Solar-grade material was an estimated 98 percent of that output and electronic-grade 2 percent8. The US Commerce Department found in 2026 that semiconductor-grade polysilicon makes up only 2.4 percent of global production, close to the electronic-grade share of China's output16. These figures do not show how much of China's electronic-grade polysilicon ends up in chips.
   - Source USGS Minerals Yearbook, China, 2023 8
   - Siltronic put the 2025 market for electronic-grade silicon at $1.4 billion, with five major suppliers, none of them Chinese: Wacker, Hemlock, OCI, Tokuyama and Mitsubishi14.
   - GlobalWafers of Taiwan has expanded its wafer making in the United States. It opened a $3.5 billion plant in Sherman, Texas in May 2025, the first fully integrated plant for advanced silicon wafers built in the United States in over twenty years15. The same day it announced another $4 billion for phases three and four in Sherman15. The US government had awarded it up to $406 million under the CHIPS incentives program for wafer plants in Texas and Missouri10.
   - US import rules issued in 2026 on polysilicon products also cover the single-crystal wafers that chips are built on. US Presidential Proclamation 11052, signed 6 August 2026, sets a minimum import price of $100 per kilogram for polysilicon ingots and wafers from 4 December 202616. From the same date it adds an extra 15 percent duty on imported ingots and other listed products made from polysilicon16. A September 2026 US Commerce Department rule meant to stop stockpiling before the duties start lists tariff code 3818.00.0040 among the products it covers25. That code covers round, doped, single-crystal silicon wafers26.
   - The US Commerce Department may approve plans to build US polysilicon, ingot, wafer or solar cell plants whose construction starts by 20 January 202916. Firms with approved plans can import production equipment, and polysilicon, ingots, wafers and solar cells in amounts the department sets to match their new investment, without the new duties16.
   - Eswin, mainland China's largest maker of 300 mm wafers, says it ranked sixth in the world with about 7.7 percent of global 300 mm shipments in the first half of 20269. Its October 2025 Shanghai listing prospectus, drawing on SEMI data, estimates that the five largest makers shipped 79.08 percent of 300 mm wafers in 202428. It puts Eswin at 6.01 percent and all other makers at 14.91 percent28.
   - Making the smoothest, high-purity 300 mm wafers takes considerable know-how that is hard to write down20. Customers have to qualify a new wafer plant before they buy from it, and Siltronic completed key customer qualifications at its new Singapore fab in July 20251.
   - The last increase in wafer size, to 300 mm, happened in 200230. Wafers are still produced in diameters of up to 300 mm2. Bigger wafers cut the cost of deposition, etching and other steps, because those steps have to be run fewer times for the same number of chips30. So printing, or photolithography, takes a bigger share of the processing cost30. A 2013 University of Texas lecture cited by CSET put photolithography at half the cost of processing a 300 mm wafer, against a quarter for a 150 mm one30.
   - Large GPU dies and the newest processes need better wafers. Some GPU dies fill the whole area a lithography tool can print in one exposure. One fatal defect on such a die ruins far more silicon than one on a small chip. Extreme-ultraviolet printing also stays in focus over only a tiny range of heights (see Lithography), so the disc has to be flat at every point. SEMI's wafer group says the move to sub-3nm processes is raising requirements for wafer quality and consistency2.
   + High concentration
   + ·
   + Moderately hard to substitute
   + Every chip is built on a wafer, a thin round slice of silicon about 30 centimeters across that carries hundreds of chips at once. Making one starts with quartz rock, which is silicon bound to oxygen1. The silicon is refined until metal impurities are no more than a few parts per billion2. It is then melted, and a small seed crystal is lowered to the surface and drawn slowly back up. The melt freezes onto the seed as one long cylinder, a single crystal with its atoms lined up in one unbroken pattern from end to end. Saws cut the cylinder into discs, and each disc is polished to a mirror finish and made extremely flat across its whole face2. In 2025 the top five wafer makers served around 75 percent of the world market, and none of them is American or Chinese3.
   + Japanese companies held 53 percent of the world's silicon wafer sales in 2023, by a Fuji Keizai survey that Japan's trade ministry reproduced4. Firms based in Taiwan, South Korea and Europe held most of the rest4. China's largest maker of 300 mm wafers, Eswin, says it ranked sixth in the world with about 7.7 percent of global 300 mm shipments in the first half of 20265. Chips also need electronic-grade polysilicon, the raw material for wafers6. The German wafer maker Siltronic named five major suppliers of it for 2025, and none is Chinese6.
   + GlobalWafers of Taiwan opened a $3.5 billion plant in Texas in May 2025, the first fully integrated plant for advanced silicon wafers built in the United States in over twenty years7. US Presidential Proclamation 11052 sets a minimum import price of $100 per kilogram for polysilicon ingots and wafers from 4 December 20268.
   + Chokepoint card
   + The world market for silicon wafers was $11.4 billion in 2025, on 12,973 million square inches shipped9. That is a small fraction of Nvidia's data center revenue, which reached $193.7 billion in the fiscal year to 25 January 202610.
   + Several firms already make 300 mm wafers that fabs have approved, but customers have to test and approve a new wafer plant before they buy from it11.
   + world wafer market in 2025, on 12,973 million square inches shipped9
   + Its parent's electronics materials segment, wafers included, had ¥1,015.7bn of sales in the year to March 202612
   + ¥409,670m of net sales in 202513
   + NT$60.6bn revenue in 202514
   + One of the top five wafer makers by revenue in the first three quarters of 20253
   + €1,346.7m revenue in 202515
   + Losing one of the top five wafer makers leaves four, and a new wafer plant can still be built3. Customer approval of a new plant is called qualification11. Siltronic completed key customer qualifications at its new Singapore fab in July 202511.
   + China made about 93 percent of the world's polysilicon in 2023, and an estimated 98 percent of China's output was solar-grade16. Eswin, mainland China's largest maker of 300 mm wafers, says it had about 7.7 percent of world 300 mm shipments in the first half of 20265.
   + None of the five largest wafer makers in 2025 was American3. GlobalWafers of Taiwan won CHIPS awards of up to $406 million in December 2024 for wafer plants in Texas and Missouri17.
   + In the next step, the Siemens process, the clean gas flows over silicon filaments heated inside a bell jar. On the hot surface the gas breaks apart and its silicon settles onto the filaments. Over days the filaments thicken into gray rods of polysilicon. Everything downstream is made from those rods. Polysilicon comes in two ultra-high-purity grades, one for semiconductors and one for solar cells, and chips use the semiconductor grade1.
   + A grower melts the polysilicon at about 1,420 degrees Celsius, hotter than lava, and pulls a single crystal out of it by the Czochralski method2. The grower lowers a small seed crystal on the end of a rod to the surface of the melt and draws it back up, slowly turning. Silicon freezes onto the seed in the seed's own atomic pattern, and a cylinder, the ingot, grows beneath it. The pull starts with a thin neck so that flaws from the seed's first contact with the melt stay in the neck and never reach the ingot. Boron or phosphorus is stirred into the melt, a step called doping. The added element sets how readily the wafer conducts and whether negative or positive charges carry its current.
   + Epitaxial wafers get one more layer of near-perfect silicon, grown on top at about 1,200 degrees Celsius2. That is still hot enough to glow orange.
   + A polished wafer is a doped single crystal polished to a mirror finish2. An epitaxial wafer is a polished wafer with a thin layer of single-crystal silicon grown on top2. The transistors are built in that layer, so flaws in it can make them leak current. SUMCO says an epitaxial wafer suits customers who need a near-perfect crystal or layers that differ in electrical resistance2. SEMI, the industry association, credits the 2025 rise in shipments to AI-driven demand for advanced epitaxial wafers for logic chips and polished wafers for high-bandwidth memory9.
   + In a silicon-on-insulator (SOI) wafer, a buried layer of oxide, which is glass, separates a thin working layer of silicon from the bulk beneath. Soitec says nearly all SOI wafers sold worldwide are made with its Smart Cut process18. Soitec's SOI product lines include RF-SOI for radio chips and fully depleted SOI (FD-SOI)18. Its markets run from mobile communications to cloud AI18. But for logic chips, SEMI credited the AI-driven growth in 2025 to a different kind of wafer, the advanced epitaxial wafer9.
   + Source METI, December 2025, from Fuji Keizai data (2023); Japan's and Europe's shares are printed, the others are read off METI's chart 4
   + Japanese companies held 53 percent of the world's silicon wafer sales in 20234. The chart shows shares of 2023 sales from a survey by Fuji Keizai, a Japanese research firm4. Japan's Ministry of Economy, Trade and Industry reproduced the figures in a December 2025 strategy deck4. The ministry's chart of the Fuji Keizai survey prints only Japan's 53 percent, so the other shares in the chart above are measured from its bars4. An earlier page of the ministry's deck prints Europe's share in the same Fuji Keizai survey as 10 percent22. The chart gives no separate share to Chinese or US companies4.
   + Siltronic's April 2026 investor presentation says the top five wafer makers serve around 75 percent of the market, based on revenue in the first three quarters of 20253. It names the five as Shin-Etsu, SUMCO, GlobalWafers, SK Siltron and Siltronic itself3. Siltronic describes itself as the only Western-based maker among the five3.
   + Shin-Etsu. Shin-Etsu Chemical's electronics materials segment, which includes its wafer business, sold ¥1,015.7 billion in the year to March 2026, up 9 percent12. AI-related demand stayed strong and demand in other sectors began to rise, lifting sales of silicon wafers, photoresist and photomask blanks12. The segment also sells rare earth magnets and other materials, and the results release does not break out wafer sales23. So the figure cannot be compared with the wafer revenue of the other firms listed.
   + SUMCO. Sold ¥409,670 million24. Its operating profit fell from ¥36,924 million to ¥1,342 million, and it ended the year with a net loss of ¥11,751 million24. In the same year it added leading-edge 300 mm capacity and reorganized production of 200 mm and smaller wafers, where demand stayed weak13.
   + Siltronic. Lost €77.9 million on €1,346.7 million of sales while investing €369.1 million, with its new Singapore fab as the focus15.
   + GlobalWafers. NT$60.6 billion of revenue, down 3.24 percent in local currency14.
   + Worldwide, the wafer area shipped rose 5.8 percent in 2025, but revenue fell 1.2 percent9. SEMI attributes the softer revenue mostly to traditional chip uses outside AI, where demand and pricing had yet to improve9.
   + Polysilicon is the raw material for both wafers and solar cells, and almost all of China's is made for solar cells. In 2023 China made 1.50 million metric tons of polysilicon, about 93 percent of world output16. Solar-grade material was an estimated 98 percent of that output and electronic-grade 2 percent16. The US Commerce Department found in 2026 that semiconductor-grade polysilicon makes up only 2.4 percent of global production, close to the electronic-grade share of China's output8. These figures do not show how much of China's electronic-grade polysilicon ends up in chips.
   + Source USGS Minerals Yearbook, China, 2023 16
   + Siltronic put the 2025 market for electronic-grade silicon at $1.4 billion, with five major suppliers, none of them Chinese: Wacker, Hemlock, OCI, Tokuyama and Mitsubishi6.
   + GlobalWafers of Taiwan has expanded its wafer making in the United States. It opened a $3.5 billion plant in Sherman, Texas in May 2025, the first fully integrated plant for advanced silicon wafers built in the United States in over twenty years7. The same day it announced another $4 billion for phases three and four in Sherman7. The US government had awarded it up to $406 million under the CHIPS incentives program for wafer plants in Texas and Missouri17.
   + US import rules issued in 2026 on polysilicon products also cover the single-crystal wafers that chips are built on. US Presidential Proclamation 11052, signed 6 August 2026, sets a minimum import price of $100 per kilogram for polysilicon ingots and wafers from 4 December 20268. From the same date it adds an extra 15 percent duty on imported ingots and other listed products made from polysilicon8. A September 2026 US Commerce Department rule meant to stop stockpiling before the duties start lists tariff code 3818.00.0040 among the products it covers25. That code covers round, doped, single-crystal silicon wafers26.
   + The US Commerce Department may approve plans to build US polysilicon, ingot, wafer or solar cell plants whose construction starts by 20 January 20298. Firms with approved plans can import production equipment, and polysilicon, ingots, wafers and solar cells in amounts the department sets to match their new investment, without the new duties8.
   + Eswin, mainland China's largest maker of 300 mm wafers, says it ranked sixth in the world with about 7.7 percent of global 300 mm shipments in the first half of 20265. Its October 2025 Shanghai listing prospectus, drawing on SEMI data, estimates that the five largest makers shipped 79.08 percent of 300 mm wafers in 202428. It puts Eswin at 6.01 percent and all other makers at 14.91 percent28.
   + Making the smoothest, high-purity 300 mm wafers takes considerable know-how that is hard to write down20. Customers have to qualify a new wafer plant before they buy from it, and Siltronic completed key customer qualifications at its new Singapore fab in July 202511.

== #chemicals-gases-and-photoresist: 333 lines before, 336 after; as printed only before 35, only after 38; marks out only before 1, only after 4; card 49 lines, the same words; order with the card set aside the same
   sources: 98 lines before, 98 after, the same set, in the new reading order
   the card before: after "A specialty gas cabinet with its door off, and a bulk liquid cylinder ", before "In plain terms"
   the card after:  after "A chip fab draws on hundreds of chemicals, gases and other materials. ", before "How it works#"

   - High
   - This rating is based on photoresist, the hardest input to replace at this stage1.
   - market for wafer fabrication materials in 20252. SEMI's count includes silicon wafers and photomasks3
   - A vehicle of the state-backed Japan Investment Corporation bought 84.36% of JSR in April 20244
   - Resist maker with ¥139,668m of sales in the first half of fiscal 20265
   - €2,465m of electronics revenue in 2025, 9.1% of group sales6
   - Bought the polishing-slurry maker CMC Materials in July 20227
   - Says it holds about 65% of the world market for semiconductor sputtering targets as of fiscal 20268
   - Each formula is tuned to the layers it prints and has to pass qualification in the fab1. So a new supplier has to develop its version together with the fab, one layer at a time9. When Japan required individual export licenses for resist and two other chemicals sold to South Korea in 2019, South Korea bought more hydrogen fluoride from the United States and Taiwan and more resist from Belgium10.
   - In March 2026 the Chinese resist maker Hubei Dinglong said China had yet to reach large-scale production of advanced resist for 248 nm, 193 nm or extreme-ultraviolet printing11. Since August 2023 China has required licenses to export gallium and germanium12.
   - In photoresist, American companies ranked a distant second to Japanese companies by sales in 202313. In 2021 JSR of Japan agreed to buy Inpria, an Oregon-based US maker of resist for extreme ultraviolet14.
   - Photoresist is a coating that changes wherever light hits it. A machine spins a coat of it, thinner than a soap bubble, across the wafer. Light shines through a stencil onto the coat, and a liquid wash then takes away the parts the light reached. What remains is a pattern, and the next machine cuts that pattern into the wafer beneath, a step called etching. Hundreds of other chemicals, gases and materials lay down layers, strip them off and clean the wafer between steps15. The cleaning chemicals are purified until metal and other impurities are down to parts per trillion, the Japanese chemical maker Sumitomo Chemical said in 202116. A chipmaker has to test and approve each new resist in its own factory before using it9. Even a resist that passes the first time takes seven to 17 months to approve, the Semiconductor Industry Association wrote in 20239. Japanese companies held 78 percent of world photoresist sales in 2023, by a Fuji Keizai survey that Japan's trade ministry reproduced13.
   - In July 2019 Japan began requiring an export license for each contract to sell photoresist, hydrogen fluoride or fluorinated polyimide to South Korea10. Japan's hydrogen fluoride exports to South Korea then fell 87.9 percent, but its photoresist exports did not10. Production of all three chemicals rose inside South Korea10.
   - China banned exports of the metals gallium, germanium and antimony to the United States in December 202417. In Europe, germanium metal averaged an estimated $4,100 a kilogram in 2025, against $1,392 in 2023, on Argus Media prices reported by the US Geological Survey18. In November 2025 China suspended the ban until 27 November 202619.
   - A chip fab draws on hundreds of chemicals, gases and other materials15. A shortage of any one of them can stop it. The French gas company Air Liquide alone supplies more than 200 different chemicals and roughly 50,000 cylinders a year6.
   - Resist makers responded with metal-oxide resists, which are built on clusters with a tin-oxide core and absorb more of the EUV light than chemically amplified resists22. In 2021 the Japanese resist maker JSR agreed to buy Inpria, an Oregon-based US maker of these resists14.
   - The other chemicals and gases put material on the wafer, take it off or keep it clean. Bulk gases such as nitrogen come by pipeline or from plants built at the fab, and specialty gases come in cylinders6. Silane gas carries the silicon for new layers into the chamber that holds the wafer. A plasma splits nitrogen trifluoride, and the fluorine it frees scrubs the chamber walls clean between wafers. Hydrofluoric acid cleans the wafer itself. Polishing slurries grind each new layer flat before the next goes on.
   - Japanese companies sell most of the world's photoresist, by two different counts. In a January 2021 brief, Georgetown University's Center for Security and Emerging Technology (CSET) put Japan's share of the semiconductor photoresist market at 90 percent, citing a 2019 estimate25. The brief put most of the rest with US and South Korean firms25. In a December 2025 strategy presentation, Japan's Ministry of Economy, Trade and Industry (METI) published figures from a separate survey by Fuji Keizai, a Japanese market research firm13. That survey counts sales by company nationality and puts Japanese companies at 77 percent in 2021 and 78 percent in 2023, with American and South Korean companies next13.
   - Source METI, December 2025, from Fuji Keizai data 13
   - In its 2025 annual report summary, the Chinese firm Hubei Dinglong said China had yet to reach large-scale production of advanced resist for 248 nm, 193 nm or EUV printing11. But it said three of its own resists for 248 nm and 193 nm printing had moved into stable volume supply28.
   - A Japanese state-backed fund, Japan Investment Corporation, took control of JSR in April 2024. A company the fund controls bought 84.36 percent of JSR's shares at ¥4,350 each in an offer to all shareholders that closed on 16 April 20244. JSR's shares were delisted from the Tokyo Stock Exchange on 25 June 202429.
   - Two Japanese suppliers tie their growth to demand for AI chips. Tokyo Ohka Kogyo sold ¥139,668 million in the first half of fiscal 2026, up 25.1 percent, as strong demand for AI servers in data centers raised demand for chips5. Fujifilm planned to raise its polishing-slurry capacity at Kumamoto in Japan by about 30 percent from January 2025, to meet demand driven by AI chips30. It also scheduled new production and quality-evaluation equipment for EUV resist at Shizuoka in Japan and Pyeongtaek in South Korea to start in October 202531.
   - World sales of wafer fabrication materials reached $45.8 billion in 2025, and sales of packaging materials reached $27.4 billion2. SEMI counts silicon wafers and photomasks among wafer fabrication materials, along with resists, gases, chemicals and polishing materials3.
   - In electronic gases, CSET named Merck, Air Products and Air Liquide as the market leaders in January 202132. Air Liquide's electronics business sold €2,465 million in 2025, 9.1 percent of group revenue6. SK Specialty has made nitrogen trifluoride since 2001, when it became the first company to produce the gas in South Korea33. The Japanese firm Kanto Denka Kogyo refines its own fluorine gas and turns it into specialty gases for deposition, etching and cleaning34. Stella Chemifa, also Japanese, makes ultra-high-purity hydrofluoric acid for chipmaking35. Another Japanese firm, Morita Chemical Industries, sells an electronic-grade hydrofluoric acid36.
   - A few firms held most of the markets for wet chemicals and polishing slurries in 2019. CSET's January 2021 brief, drawing on a 2019 report by the market research firm Mordor Intelligence, put five firms, KMG Chemicals, BASF, Avantor, Honeywell and Kanto Chemical, at more than 60 percent of the wet chemicals market32. Citing a 2019 report by the market research firm Market Reports World, it put DuPont and Cabot Microelectronics at about 56 percent of a $790 million market for polishing slurries25. Counting by company nationality, the same Fuji Keizai survey, as published by METI, puts Japanese companies at 51 percent of polishing-slurry sales and 59 percent of high-purity cleaning-liquid sales in 202313. Both shares were 49 percent in 202113.
   - The slurry businesses of both leaders CSET named have since moved to other companies. Cabot Microelectronics renamed itself CMC Materials in October 202037. The American firm Entegris completed its purchase of CMC Materials on 6 July 20227. DuPont separated its electronics business into an independent company, Qnity Electronics, on 1 November 202538. Qnity sells polishing slurries and pads39.
   - In sputtering targets for semiconductors, JX Advanced Metals puts its own share of the world market at about 65 percent as of fiscal 20268.
   - In July 2019 Japan put photoresist, hydrogen fluoride and fluorinated polyimide under individual export licenses for South Korea, so each export contract needed its own license10. South Korea's chip industry depended heavily on Japan for all three chemicals, and Japanese firms supplied more than 90 percent of South Korea's imports of two of them10. Japan's hydrogen fluoride exports to South Korea then fell 87.9 percent10. Japanese photoresist exports to South Korea did not fall, possibly in part because Japan allowed three-year bulk licenses for some resist sales in December 201910.
   - South Korea bought more hydrogen fluoride from the United States and Taiwan and more photoresist from Belgium10. Production of all three chemicals rose inside South Korea10. The rise came both from South Korean firms and from Japanese companies' affiliates in South Korea10.
   - Researchers at Japan's Research Institute of Economy, Trade and Industry (RIETI) found that Japanese hydrogen fluoride exports to the United States rose10. They say some Japanese supply may have reached South Korea by way of the United States10. Japan ended the individual licenses on 23 March 2023, when it put exports of the three chemicals to South Korea under its bulk export license system40.
   - For chipmaking materials, China's export controls cover metals. On 3 July 2023 China announced that exports of gallium and germanium would need licenses from 1 August12. On 3 December 2024 it said it would in principle grant no licenses to export gallium, germanium, antimony or superhard materials to the United States17. On 9 November 2025 it suspended that ban until 27 November 202619. In February 2026 the US Geological Survey put China's share of world production of primary low-purity gallium at 99 percent43.
   - Most gallium used in the United States goes into chips, and both metals are recovered as byproducts of processing other minerals. Of the gallium used in the United States in 2025, 73 percent went into integrated circuits built on gallium arsenide or gallium nitride43. Another 26 percent went into optoelectronic devices such as LEDs and laser diodes43. In the United States, germanium goes mainly into fiber optics, infrared optics, semiconductors and solar cells, and radiation detectors18. Gallium is recovered mostly as a byproduct of processing bauxite, the ore that aluminum is made from43. Germanium is recovered from certain zinc smelter residues and from coal ash45. Because both metals are economical to produce only as byproducts, their supply may not rise much when their prices do45.
   - In Europe, germanium metal averaged an estimated $4,100 a kilogram in 2025, against $1,392 in 2023, on Argus Media prices reported by the US Geological Survey18. US imports of the metal fell an estimated 67 percent in 2025 because of China's ban18. In October 2024 the US Geological Survey estimated that a total Chinese ban on gallium and germanium exports could cut US GDP by $3.4 billion46.
   - Source USGS Mineral Commodity Summaries, February 2026 (Argus Media European prices, 2025 estimated) 18
   - Each resist made without PFAS would need full qualification in the chipmaker's fab9. The Semiconductor Industry Association wrote in 2023 that photoresist needs the most rigorous qualification of any chemical a chipmaker uses, because each resist is tuned to the layers it prints1. The resist maker adjusts its formula over rounds of tests in the fab9. A fab could not qualify all the new resists at once, mainly because testing several together can muddle the yield results9. So some would have to be qualified one after another9.

   + Two Japanese suppliers tie their growth to demand for AI chips. Tokyo Ohka Kogyo sold ¥139,668 million in the first half of fiscal 2026, up 25.1 percent, as strong demand for AI servers in data centers raised demand for chips14. Fujifilm planned to raise its polishing-slurry capacity at Kumamoto in Japan by about 30 percent from January 2025, to meet demand driven by AI chips30. It also scheduled new production and quality-evaluation equipment for EUV resist at Shizuoka in Japan and Pyeongtaek in South Korea to start in October 202531.
   + World sales of wafer fabrication materials reached $45.8 billion in 2025, and sales of packaging materials reached $27.4 billion11. SEMI counts silicon wafers and photomasks among wafer fabrication materials, along with resists, gases, chemicals and polishing materials12.
   + In electronic gases, CSET named Merck, Air Products and Air Liquide as the market leaders in January 202132. Air Liquide's electronics business sold €2,465 million in 2025, 9.1 percent of group revenue9. SK Specialty has made nitrogen trifluoride since 2001, when it became the first company to produce the gas in South Korea33. The Japanese firm Kanto Denka Kogyo refines its own fluorine gas and turns it into specialty gases for deposition, etching and cleaning34. Stella Chemifa, also Japanese, makes ultra-high-purity hydrofluoric acid for chipmaking35. Another Japanese firm, Morita Chemical Industries, sells an electronic-grade hydrofluoric acid36.
   + A few firms held most of the markets for wet chemicals and polishing slurries in 2019. CSET's January 2021 brief, drawing on a 2019 report by the market research firm Mordor Intelligence, put five firms, KMG Chemicals, BASF, Avantor, Honeywell and Kanto Chemical, at more than 60 percent of the wet chemicals market32. Citing a 2019 report by the market research firm Market Reports World, it put DuPont and Cabot Microelectronics at about 56 percent of a $790 million market for polishing slurries25. Counting by company nationality, the same Fuji Keizai survey, as published by METI, puts Japanese companies at 51 percent of polishing-slurry sales and 59 percent of high-purity cleaning-liquid sales in 20234. Both shares were 49 percent in 20214.
   + The slurry businesses of both leaders CSET named have since moved to other companies. Cabot Microelectronics renamed itself CMC Materials in October 202037. The American firm Entegris completed its purchase of CMC Materials on 6 July 202215. DuPont separated its electronics business into an independent company, Qnity Electronics, on 1 November 202538. Qnity sells polishing slurries and pads39.
   + In sputtering targets for semiconductors, JX Advanced Metals puts its own share of the world market at about 65 percent as of fiscal 202616.
   + In July 2019 Japan put photoresist, hydrogen fluoride and fluorinated polyimide under individual export licenses for South Korea, so each export contract needed its own license5. South Korea's chip industry depended heavily on Japan for all three chemicals, and Japanese firms supplied more than 90 percent of South Korea's imports of two of them5. Japan's hydrogen fluoride exports to South Korea then fell 87.9 percent5. Japanese photoresist exports to South Korea did not fall, possibly in part because Japan allowed three-year bulk licenses for some resist sales in December 20195.
   + South Korea bought more hydrogen fluoride from the United States and Taiwan and more photoresist from Belgium5. Production of all three chemicals rose inside South Korea5. The rise came both from South Korean firms and from Japanese companies' affiliates in South Korea5.
   + Researchers at Japan's Research Institute of Economy, Trade and Industry (RIETI) found that Japanese hydrogen fluoride exports to the United States rose5. They say some Japanese supply may have reached South Korea by way of the United States5. Japan ended the individual licenses on 23 March 2023, when it put exports of the three chemicals to South Korea under its bulk export license system40.
   + For chipmaking materials, China's export controls cover metals. On 3 July 2023 China announced that exports of gallium and germanium would need licenses from 1 August18. On 3 December 2024 it said it would in principle grant no licenses to export gallium, germanium, antimony or superhard materials to the United States6. On 9 November 2025 it suspended that ban until 27 November 20268. In February 2026 the US Geological Survey put China's share of world production of primary low-purity gallium at 99 percent43.
   + Most gallium used in the United States goes into chips, and both metals are recovered as byproducts of processing other minerals. Of the gallium used in the United States in 2025, 73 percent went into integrated circuits built on gallium arsenide or gallium nitride43. Another 26 percent went into optoelectronic devices such as LEDs and laser diodes43. In the United States, germanium goes mainly into fiber optics, infrared optics, semiconductors and solar cells, and radiation detectors7. Gallium is recovered mostly as a byproduct of processing bauxite, the ore that aluminum is made from43. Germanium is recovered from certain zinc smelter residues and from coal ash45. Because both metals are economical to produce only as byproducts, their supply may not rise much when their prices do45.
   + In Europe, germanium metal averaged an estimated $4,100 a kilogram in 2025, against $1,392 in 2023, on Argus Media prices reported by the US Geological Survey7. US imports of the metal fell an estimated 67 percent in 2025 because of China's ban7. In October 2024 the US Geological Survey estimated that a total Chinese ban on gallium and germanium exports could cut US GDP by $3.4 billion46.
   + Source USGS Mineral Commodity Summaries, February 2026 (Argus Media European prices, 2025 estimated) 7
   + Each resist made without PFAS would need full qualification in the chipmaker's fab3. The Semiconductor Industry Association wrote in 2023 that photoresist needs the most rigorous qualification of any chemical a chipmaker uses, because each resist is tuned to the layers it prints10. The resist maker adjusts its formula over rounds of tests in the fab3. A fab could not qualify all the new resists at once, mainly because testing several together can muddle the yield results3. So some would have to be qualified one after another3.
== #lithography: 402 lines before, 405 after; as printed only before 32, only after 35; marks out only before 1, only after 4; card 40 lines, the same words; order with the card set aside the same
   sources: 98 lines before, 98 after, the same set, in the new reading order
   the card before: after "Wafer: see stage 02, Silicon and Wafers", before "In plain terms"
   the card after:  after "ASML sold 327 lithography systems in 2025, 48 of them extreme ultravio", before "How it works#"
   words only before: Extreme
   words only after:  Extreme concentration
   words only after:  ·
   words only after:  Very hard to substitute
   words only after:  Chokepoint card
   - Extreme
   - ASML is the only maker of EUV scanners, and Zeiss is its sole supplier of their mirrors1.
   - for a standard EUV scanner, an estimated $350-400M for the newer High-NA version and roughly $60M for a tool in the DUV segment, according to Reuters in July 20262
   - 22 new chip lithography systems in the year to March 20264
   - 234 chip lithography systems in 2025, 188 of them i-line and 46 KrF5
   - A newcomer would first have to make mirrors a meter wide, polished smooth to within tens of picometers, a picometer being a trillionth of a meter6. It would also need a light source that hits tin droplets with a laser 50,000 times a second7.
   - In September 2025 CSIS judged that EUV remained firmly out of reach for China despite massive state investment8. SMEE, a Chinese scanner maker, had its largest share in i-line tools, the coarsest kind, and even there held 4 percent of the world market as of 20258.
   - No American firm makes scanners, CSET found in 20219. But a US rule published in October 2023 covers immersion scanners with an alignment error between 1.5 nm and 2.4 nm bound for advanced chipmaking if they hold any American-made parts10. The US entity list also requires a license to supply the firms it names11.
   - Lithography prints the pattern of a circuit onto the wafer. A machine holds a stencil of one layer of the circuit up to a light source and projects the image, four times smaller, onto the silicon. The shorter the wavelength of the light, the finer the lines a machine can print. Even so, a machine can print lines narrower than its light's wavelength. Immersion scanners, for example, shine the light through a layer of water between lens and wafer, which lets them print smaller features with the same light6. ASML rates its NXT:2150i immersion scanner at features of 38 nanometers or less with 193-nanometer argon fluoride laser light12. Air soaks up extreme ultraviolet light, the shortest in use, so machines that print with it work in a vacuum. Only ASML, a Dutch company, makes them. Export controls are government rules on who a company may sell to. Every advanced chip passes through ASML's machines, so export controls on ASML affect all advanced chipmaking.
   - ASML is the only company that makes extreme ultraviolet (EUV) scanners3. It also holds above 80 percent of the market for deep ultraviolet (DUV) lithography, which uses light of longer wavelength than EUV2. ASML says the production capacity of Zeiss, its sole supplier of optics, limits how many lithography systems it can build1.
   - Export controls set up by the United States with allies including the Netherlands block EUV scanners from Chinese chip foundries8. In December 2025 Reuters reported a Chinese prototype that had made EUV light but had printed no chip13. In July 2026 Reuters reported, citing one unnamed source, that a Shanghai firm with two state shareholders has started producing immersion DUV tools, which shine their light through water14.
   - ASML sold 327 lithography systems in 2025, 48 of them extreme ultraviolet (EUV)15. Nobody else makes EUV systems3.
   - Finer lines need shorter light. Glass and air both absorb the shortest light in use, at 13.5 nm20. So an EUV scanner works in a vacuum and uses mirrors instead of lenses, and its masks are mirrors too20. Deep ultraviolet (DUV) machines use light with a wavelength more than fourteen times longer7. ASML calls its immersion DUV scanners the workhorses of the industry, used in high-volume manufacturing of the most advanced logic and memory chips21.
   - Deep ultraviolet (DUV) light comes from gas lasers called excimer lasers: 248 nanometers from krypton fluoride (KrF), 193 from argon fluoride (ArF), shone through air or water7. Cymer, an independently run ASML business in San Diego, makes these excimer lasers, which generate the light that scanners use to print patterns on wafers24. Gigaphoton, a Komatsu company in Japan, also makes excimer lasers for lithography tools25.
   - Of the systems ASML sold in 2025, 131 were ArF immersion, 78 KrF and 16 ArF dry, all deep ultraviolet, and another 54 were older i-line tools26. The i-line tools use 365 nm light from a mercury lamp, and ASML says they print features as small as 220 nm7.
   - Reuters gives one price for the whole DUV segment, with tools costing roughly $60 million each2. ASML's own figures split its DUV sales by tool type. In 2025 the 131 ArF immersion tools it sold made up 42 percent of its system sales, and the 78 KrF tools only 4 percent26. In DUV, ASML competes with Nikon and Canon, and it still holds above 80 percent of that market2.
   - The 13.5 nm light comes from molten tin droplets shot from a generator7. A laser hits each droplet twice, first with a low-intensity pulse that flattens it and then with a more powerful one that vaporizes it into plasma7. The process repeats 50,000 times a second7. Mirrors made by Zeiss, a German optics company, carry the light to the wafer6. The largest are a meter across and smooth to within tens of picometers, a picometer being a thousandth of a nanometer6.
   - Reuters puts a standard EUV tool at around $200 million and a High-NA machine at an estimated $350 to $400 million2.
   - Source Reuters explainer on ASML's lithography machines, first published 28 January 2026 and updated 28 July 2026 2
   - ASML sold four High-NA (EXE) systems in 2025, against two in 202431. In its 2025 annual report, filed in February 2026, ASML said it expected High-NA to start supporting high-volume manufacturing in 20271.
   - Source ASML 2025 annual report (2024 and 2025); 2023 from ASML's Q4 2023 investor presentation 15
   - ASML's net sales were EUR 32.7 billion in 2025, at a 52.8 percent gross margin15. In October 2026 it expected 2026 net sales of EUR 44 to 45 billion, at a gross margin of around 56 percent35. Its 2025 annual report lists 5,100 suppliers1. Three of them and ASML's own San Diego site make the main parts of the EUV machine:
   - ASML says the production capacity of Zeiss, its sole supplier of optics, limits how many lithography systems it can build1.
   - In Japan, Nikon and Canon also make lithography tools. Nikon sold 22 new chip lithography systems in the year to March 2026, in a market it sizes at 570 units for 20254. Its Precision Equipment business made an operating loss in the year to March 202639. Canon sold 234 chip lithography systems in 2025, 188 of them i-line and 46 KrF5. Its range runs from KrF scanners to i-line steppers, including some built for advanced packaging40. Canon also makes a nanoimprint system, the FPA-1200NZ2C, which stamps the pattern instead of projecting it and can print lines down to 14 nm wide41. Canon delivered one in 2024 to the Texas Institute for Electronics, to be used for research and prototypes41.
   - In January 2021 CSET, a research center at Georgetown University, found that the United States lacked firms in a few key parts of the chip supply chain, above all lithography tools9.
   - Export controls set up by the United States with allies including the Netherlands block ASML from shipping EUV scanners to Chinese foundries8. In September 2025 CSIS judged that EUV remained "firmly out of reach" for China despite massive state investment8. For DUV scanners and other tools, the restrictions came in this order:
   - A US rule published on 25 October 2023 covered immersion scanners that print features of 45 nm or smaller with an alignment error between 1.5 nm and 2.4 nm10. If such a scanner was bound for advanced chipmaking, the rule put it under US export rules when it held any American content at all10. The rule did not apply to scanners first exported from a country that lists them on its own export control list10.
   - The BIS Affiliates Rule, issued by the US Bureau of Industry and Security, extended entity-list controls to affiliates owned 50 percent or more by listed entities from 29 September 202511. BIS then suspended it until 9 November 202645. Exporting goods covered by US export rules to a firm on the entity list needs a US license11.
   - The EU added certain Dutch controls to the EU Control List in November 20251. ASML says this changed nothing for its immersion tools, because they already needed a Dutch license1.
   - On DUV, Reuters reported in July 2026, citing one unnamed source, that Shanghai Aishengna Electronic Technology Group has started producing home-grown immersion DUV tools14. The firm was registered in August 2023 with 7 billion yuan of capital and two state shareholders, Shanghai Electric Holding and a Shanghai International Trust subsidiary14. It has no website and has taken in teams from SMEE, China's older scanner maker, and the startup Yuliangsheng14. SMEE is strongest in i-line, the coarsest kind of lithography tool, and even there holds only four percent of the world market8.
   - There is less evidence of Chinese progress on EUV. Reuters reported in December 2025 that a Shenzhen team of former ASML engineers had built a prototype that made extreme ultraviolet light but had printed no chip13. To print a chip, a machine also needs mirrors that carry that light to the wafer, and ASML's sole supplier of those mirrors is Zeiss1.
   - If ASML stopped shipping, no fab anywhere could add EUV capacity, because no other company makes EUV scanners3. The scanners already in fabs would keep printing only as long as they were serviced. ASML has around 10,000 customer support employees who maintain and upgrade those machines around the clock46. Serviced this way, ASML's machines stay in use for a long time. In its 2025 annual report ASML said about 95 percent of the systems it had sold in the previous 30 years were still in use1. The same report warned that export controls may limit the service ASML can provide for machines already installed47.

== #photomasks-and-pellicles: 278 lines before, 281 after; as printed only before 30, only after 33; marks out only before 1, only after 4; card 49 lines, the same words; order with the card set aside the same
   sources: 68 lines before, 68 after, the same set, in the new reading order
   the card before: after "An EUV photomask, or reticle, with its pellicle dust cover. The glass ", before "In plain terms"
   the card after:  after "Each EUV layer of an AI accelerator is printed from a mask built on a ", before "How it works#"
   words only before: Extreme
   words only after:  Extreme concentration
   words only after:  ·
   words only after:  Hard to substitute
   words only after:  Chokepoint card
   - Extreme
   - AGC said in December 2024 that only three firms supply the blank plates for EUV masks1.
   - for a leading-edge mask set, SemiAnalysis's May 2026 figure2. In July 2022 it put a 7 nm set above $10M3
   - Ranks itself No.2 worldwide in EUV mask blanks on its own fiscal 2026 estimates4
   - Said in its 2026 report that its share of mask blanks is exceptionally high5
   - Launched the world's first tool that inspects patterned EUV masks at the scanner's own wavelength in 20196
   - CSET puts its 2025 share of the market for all electron-beam mask writers at 46.6%, using TechInsights data7
   - $849.3M of revenue in fiscal 20258
   - AGC says it spent more than a decade meeting the EUV blank's requirements, such as near-zero thermal expansion, and began mass production only in 20171. Germany's Zeiss calls its AIMS the only system that tests how a mask will print under conditions equivalent to the scanner's9.
   - As of 2026 SMIC runs two mask shops of its own in Shanghai10. No Chinese firm appears in CSET's 2025 market shares for electron-beam mask writers, the machines that draw the pattern onto a mask7.
   - In December 2025 Photronics called itself a leading worldwide maker of photomasks8. As of 2026 KLA sells tools that inspect EUV masks and optical masks, the quartz masks used with deep ultraviolet light11.
   - A photomask is the stencil that lithography prints from, at least one for each layer of a chip12. The same mask prints its layer on wafer after wafer, so one mask shapes millions of chips. The most complicated layers are printed with extreme ultraviolet (EUV) light, and the rest can often be printed with deep ultraviolet light13. For deep ultraviolet the mask is a quartz plate that the light shines through. No glass lets EUV light through, so the mask for EUV layers is a mirror. The mirror is built up from about forty pairs of thin layers, and the light bounces off it. Japan's AGC and Hoya are two of the three firms that make these mirrors. A dark pattern drawn on top of the mirror soaks up the light wherever no line should print. Any speck of dust on the mask repeats on every wafer it prints. So a thin membrane on a frame, called a pellicle, sits just above the mask and catches dust where it is too far out of focus to print.
   - On CSET's count from TechInsights data, Austria's IMS Nanofabrication held 46.6 percent of the 2025 world market for electron-beam mask writers, which draw the pattern onto a mask7. Hoya says its share of the mask blank market is exceptionally high5.
   - SemiAnalysis put a full set of masks for a leading-edge chip at $40 million in May 20262. In July 2022 it wrote that more companies will lack the volume to spread the fixed cost of mask sets3. On 8 September 2026 ASML and TSMC announced a plan to move from 6-inch to 12-inch masks14. They expect the change to raise fab productivity and lower chipmaking costs14.
   - Each EUV layer of an AI accelerator is printed from a mask built on a blank plate. Only three companies worldwide supply those plates, and Japan's AGC is one of them1. Hoya, also Japanese, is another5. AGC says it is the only company in the world that takes an EUV blank all the way from the glass to the finished coating15.
   - On 8 September 2026 ASML and TSMC announced a plan to move from the current 6-inch masks to 12-inch masks14. They expect the change to raise fab productivity, lower chipmaking costs and remove stitching constraints, the design limits that come from printing a large chip in two halves14. They aim to set up a 12-inch mask pilot line by 2031, so that High-NA scanners using 12-inch masks can enter advanced chip production by 203314.
   - AGC says it spent more than a decade meeting the blank's requirements, which include near-zero thermal expansion, perfectly smooth and flat surfaces and no particle contamination1. It says it began mass production only in 20171.
   - AGC publishes no market share figure for EUV blanks. In its data book, AGC ranks itself No.2 worldwide in EUV blanks, based on its own fiscal 2026 estimates4. Hoya says its share of the mask blank market is exceptionally high, and it calls itself the primary supplier for leading-edge EUV nodes5. It also says customers may gradually start buying EUV blanks from more than one supplier5.
   - AGC planned to add EUV blank capacity by 2025, and it says its sales reached their target a year early. In April 2023 AGC said its EUV blank capacity would grow by about 30 percent in 202515. The growth would come from a line in Fukushima15. METI, Japan's industry ministry, chose the project for a program that supports new plants and equipment in Japan to strengthen supply chains15. AGC also said it was aiming for more than ¥40 billion of sales in the business by 202515. Its EUV blank sales grew 50 percent in 2023, AGC said in December 20241. In February 2025 AGC reported that the sales reached ¥40 billion in 2024, which it called a year ahead of its initial target21.
   - In September 2023 Intel, the majority owner of Austria's IMS Nanofabrication, called it the established industry leader in the multi-beam mask writing tools that advanced EUV requires24. Counting all electron-beam mask writers, CSET, a research center at Georgetown University, put IMS's share of a $994 million world market at 46.6 percent in 2025, using TechInsights data7. NuFlare had 39.2 percent and JEOL 14.2 percent, in the same CSET analysis of TechInsights data7.
   - A mask defect cuts the number of good chips on every wafer that mask prints, so masks are checked as blanks, after patterning and again at the fab. Actinic inspection uses 13.5 nm light, the wavelength of EUV lithography6. In 2019 Lasertec launched the ACTIS A150, which it calls the world's first actinic inspection system for patterned EUV masks6. In October 2025 it launched the ACTIS A200HiT series, which it says triples the A150's inspection speed6. Lasertec reported net sales of ¥230,485 million in the year to June 202627.
   - As of 2026 KLA's Teron systems for mask shops detect pattern and particle defects on both EUV masks and optical masks, the quartz masks used with deep ultraviolet light11.
   - Zeiss makes AIMS EUV, which checks a mask in EUV light under the scanner's own imaging conditions to judge how a defect will affect printing9. Zeiss calls AIMS the only system that tests how a mask will print under conditions equivalent to the scanner's9. Zeiss says the tool can be extended to ASML's High-NA scanner, the EXE:50009. Zeiss also sells electron-beam repair tools that work on EUV masks28.
   - A mask set costs more at each newer node, by SemiAnalysis's figures. In July 2022 SemiAnalysis put mask sets above $1 million at 28 nm and above $10 million at 7 nm3. It expected 3 nm mask sets to reach the $40 million range3. Its May 2026 primer still puts a leading-edge mask set at $40 million2. The same primer puts a single respin, remaking a chip after a design flaw, at $50 million to $100 million at leading-edge nodes2.
   - Mask costs restrict which firms can use a node. SemiAnalysis wrote that at 5 nm and 3 nm, only firms with large wafer volume see the cost per transistor fall3. It wrote that more companies will lack the volume to spread the fixed cost of mask sets3. It expected failed chip designs to show up first among AI chip startups, which work on much tighter budgets3.
   - Source SemiAnalysis, The Dark Side of the Semiconductor Design Renaissance, July 2022. SemiAnalysis's May 2026 EDA Market Primer puts a leading-edge mask set at $40M 3
   - Mask making splits between chipmakers that make masks for their own use and independent merchant firms that sell to them and others12. Photronics says that chipmakers' own shops once supplied most of the market, and that production has more recently tended to move toward the independents12. TSMC makes masks too, and lists mask making among the services it offers besides making wafers34.
   - Photronics calls itself a leading worldwide maker of masks for chips and flat panel displays8. It had $849.3 million of revenue in fiscal 2025, $615.1 million of it from chip masks8. The competitors it names in chip and display masks include Dai Nippon Printing, Hoya, Tekscend Photomask and two Chinese firms, Shenzhen Newway Photomask and Shenzhen Qingyi Photomask12. It also competes with chipmakers' own mask shops12.
   - Chinese and foreign firms make masks in China, but no Chinese firm appears in CSET's 2025 market shares for electron-beam mask writers, the machines that draw the pattern onto a mask7. SMIC, a Chinese foundry, runs two in-house mask shops in Shanghai that make masks for deep ultraviolet and older lithography machines10. Photronics began operating two mask plants in China, in Hefei and Xiamen, in 201912. Its chip mask business in Xiamen is a joint venture in which Dai Nippon Printing holds 49.99 percent12.
   - Every EUV mask depends on a handful of firms at each step, from the blank plate to the tool that tests how it will print. Only three firms supply the blank plates for EUV masks1. IMS Nanofabrication held 46.6 percent of the 2025 market for electron-beam mask writers and NuFlare 39.2 percent, by CSET's count7. In 2019 Lasertec launched what it calls the first tool that inspects patterned EUV masks in EUV light6. Zeiss calls its AIMS the only system that tests how a mask will print under conditions equivalent to the scanner's9.
   + Extreme concentration
   + ·
   + Hard to substitute
   + A photomask is the stencil that lithography prints from, at least one for each layer of a chip1. The same mask prints its layer on wafer after wafer, so one mask shapes millions of chips. The most complicated layers are printed with extreme ultraviolet (EUV) light, and the rest can often be printed with deep ultraviolet light2. For deep ultraviolet the mask is a quartz plate that the light shines through. No glass lets EUV light through, so the mask for EUV layers is a mirror. The mirror is built up from about forty pairs of thin layers, and the light bounces off it. Japan's AGC and Hoya are two of the three firms that make these mirrors. A dark pattern drawn on top of the mirror soaks up the light wherever no line should print. Any speck of dust on the mask repeats on every wafer it prints. So a thin membrane on a frame, called a pellicle, sits just above the mask and catches dust where it is too far out of focus to print.
   + On CSET's count from TechInsights data, Austria's IMS Nanofabrication held 46.6 percent of the 2025 world market for electron-beam mask writers, which draw the pattern onto a mask3. Hoya says its share of the mask blank market is exceptionally high4.
   + SemiAnalysis put a full set of masks for a leading-edge chip at $40 million in May 20265. In July 2022 it wrote that more companies will lack the volume to spread the fixed cost of mask sets6. On 8 September 2026 ASML and TSMC announced a plan to move from 6-inch to 12-inch masks7. They expect the change to raise fab productivity and lower chipmaking costs7.
   + Chokepoint card
   + Each EUV layer of an AI accelerator is printed from a mask built on a blank plate. Only three companies worldwide supply those plates, and Japan's AGC is one of them8. Hoya, also Japanese, is another4. AGC says it is the only company in the world that takes an EUV blank all the way from the glass to the finished coating9.
   + AGC said in December 2024 that only three firms supply the blank plates for EUV masks8.
