   + AGC says it spent more than a decade meeting the EUV blank's requirements, such as near-zero thermal expansion, and began mass production only in 20178. Germany's Zeiss calls its AIMS the only system that tests how a mask will print under conditions equivalent to the scanner's13.
   + As of 2026 SMIC runs two mask shops of its own in Shanghai14. No Chinese firm appears in CSET's 2025 market shares for electron-beam mask writers, the machines that draw the pattern onto a mask3.
   + In December 2025 Photronics called itself a leading worldwide maker of photomasks12. As of 2026 KLA sells tools that inspect EUV masks and optical masks, the quartz masks used with deep ultraviolet light15.
   + On 8 September 2026 ASML and TSMC announced a plan to move from the current 6-inch masks to 12-inch masks7. They expect the change to raise fab productivity, lower chipmaking costs and remove stitching constraints, the design limits that come from printing a large chip in two halves7. They aim to set up a 12-inch mask pilot line by 2031, so that High-NA scanners using 12-inch masks can enter advanced chip production by 20337.
   + AGC says it spent more than a decade meeting the blank's requirements, which include near-zero thermal expansion, perfectly smooth and flat surfaces and no particle contamination8. It says it began mass production only in 20178.
   + AGC publishes no market share figure for EUV blanks. In its data book, AGC ranks itself No.2 worldwide in EUV blanks, based on its own fiscal 2026 estimates10. Hoya says its share of the mask blank market is exceptionally high, and it calls itself the primary supplier for leading-edge EUV nodes4. It also says customers may gradually start buying EUV blanks from more than one supplier4.
   + AGC planned to add EUV blank capacity by 2025, and it says its sales reached their target a year early. In April 2023 AGC said its EUV blank capacity would grow by about 30 percent in 20259. The growth would come from a line in Fukushima9. METI, Japan's industry ministry, chose the project for a program that supports new plants and equipment in Japan to strengthen supply chains9. AGC also said it was aiming for more than ¥40 billion of sales in the business by 20259. Its EUV blank sales grew 50 percent in 2023, AGC said in December 20248. In February 2025 AGC reported that the sales reached ¥40 billion in 2024, which it called a year ahead of its initial target21.
   + In September 2023 Intel, the majority owner of Austria's IMS Nanofabrication, called it the established industry leader in the multi-beam mask writing tools that advanced EUV requires24. Counting all electron-beam mask writers, CSET, a research center at Georgetown University, put IMS's share of a $994 million world market at 46.6 percent in 2025, using TechInsights data3. NuFlare had 39.2 percent and JEOL 14.2 percent, in the same CSET analysis of TechInsights data3.

In October 2025 it launched the ACTIS A200HiT series, which it says triples the A150's inspection speed11. Lasertec reported net sales of ¥230,485 million in the year to June 202627.

== #deposition-and-etch: 386 lines before, 389 after; as printed only before 28, only after 31; marks out only before 1, only after 4; card 49 lines, the same words; order with the card set aside the same
   sources: 96 lines before, 96 after, the same set, in the new reading order
   the card before: after "A plasma etch chamber cut in half. The vessel is 500 mm across inside ", before "In plain terms"
   the card after:  after "Applied Materials and Lam Research are the two largest deposition and ", before "How it works#"
   words only before: High
   words only after:  High concentration
   words only after:  ·
   words only after:  Moderately hard to substitute
   words only after:  Chokepoint card
   - High
   - In 2025 Lam Research held 41.1 percent of dry etch tools, Tokyo Electron 23.4 percent and Applied Materials 17.4 percent, by CSET's count from TechInsights data1.
   - deposition tool market in 2025, by CSET's count from TechInsights data2. Dry etch tools were a $21.9B market in 20251
   - 82.4% of physical vapor deposition tools in 2025, by CSET's count from TechInsights data3
   - 51.2% of conductor etch tools in 20254
   - 55.9% of dielectric etch tools in 20255
   - 14.7% of physical vapor deposition tools in 20253
   - Buyers have alternatives7. At least five firms sell atomic layer deposition tools, which lay down a film a fraction of a layer of atoms at a time6.
   - Chinese toolmakers held 12.4 percent of dry etch in 2025, according to CSET's analysis of TechInsights data1. They held 2.6 percent of atomic layer deposition that year, by the same count6.
   - A December 2024 US rule lists some dry etch, atomic layer deposition and deep-hole deposition tools as controlled exports8. Its foreign direct product rule reaches some tools built outside the United States9.
   - Deposition and etch build the chip itself, one layer at a time. Deposition lays down a thin film, sometimes only a few atoms thick. Lithography prints a pattern on it. Etch then eats away everything the pattern does not protect, and the next film goes down on top. Making one chip takes 1,000 to 1,500 steps, the Semiconductor Industry Association estimated in 202510. Some of the holes the etch cuts in memory chips are more than 50 times deeper than they are wide, like a finger-wide shaft about a meter deep11. The film has to coat them right to the bottom. The etch has to eat one material and leave the one beside it untouched. Applied Materials and Lam Research in the United States, Tokyo Electron in Japan and ASM International in the Netherlands make most of these machines. Chinese toolmakers have gained share in both kinds of tool since 2019.
   - American companies held 60.3 percent of the world market for deposition tools in 2025, Japanese companies 14.0 percent and China-based companies 9.8 percent, by CSET's count from TechInsights data2. In etch and cleaning tools that year, American companies held 52.6 percent, Japanese companies 32.6 percent and China-based companies 10.1 percent12.
   - Atomic layer deposition tools coat a hole's walls and floor as evenly as its rim7. China-based companies held only 2.6 percent of the world market for them in 2025, by CSET's count from TechInsights data6. The December 2024 US export rule targets tools for atomic layer deposition of the metals molybdenum and ruthenium, and for coating holes more than 200 times deeper than they are wide13.
   - Applied Materials and Lam Research are the two largest deposition and etch suppliers. Applied Materials' revenue across all its businesses was $28.368 billion in fiscal 202514. Lam Research's was $23.233 billion in fiscal 2026, the year to June 202615.
   - To coat the floor of a deep hole as thickly as its rim, the machine can work in cycles, a method called atomic layer deposition. First one gas settles in a single layer on every surface, and the pump removes the rest. Then a second gas reacts with that layer and nothing else, and the pump empties the chamber again. Each cycle adds a fraction of a layer of atoms wherever the gas reached, so the film grows as evenly on a hole's walls and floor as on its rim7.
   - The market sizes and leading shares below are 2025 figures from Georgetown University's Center for Security and Emerging Technology (CSET), based on data from the research firm TechInsights. CSET puts the deposition tool market at $26.2 billion2. It puts the dry etch market at $21.9 billion1.
   - Physical vapor deposition (PVD), which knocks atoms off a solid target and lands them on the wafer, was a $4.9 billion market in 20253. Applied Materials held 82.4 percent of it and China's Naura 14.7 percent3.
   - Atomic layer deposition (ALD) lays down less than one atomic layer at a time. Each step stops once every surface is covered, so it coats vertical walls as evenly as horizontal floors. The US Bureau of Industry and Security (BIS), which writes the export rules, says that even coating makes it important for building 3D structures, such as 3D DRAM, 3D NAND, and the FinFET and gate-all-around transistors in logic chips7. ASM International leads it with 54.5 percent of a $4.1 billion market in 20256.
   - Conductor etch, which cuts gates, metals and silicon, was a $12.2 billion market in 2025, led by Lam at 51.2 percent, by CSET's count from TechInsights data4.
   - Dielectric etch, which cuts the insulating oxides and nitrides between the conductors, was an $8.7 billion market in 2025, led by Tokyo Electron at 55.9 percent, by CSET's count from TechInsights data5.
   - HBM (high-bandwidth memory) needs holes etched straight through the silicon, called through-silicon vias (TSVs), so chips can be stacked and wired25. BIS controls the tools that etch these holes at aspect ratios (depth to width) of 10:1 or more and at rates above 7 microns a minute8. The control covers only tools that also keep the holes' depth even to within 2 percent across the wafer8.
   - No one firm dominates deposition and etch the way ASML dominates lithography. In dry etch, CSET's count for 2025 from TechInsights data puts Lam Research at 41.1 percent, Tokyo Electron at 23.4 percent and Applied Materials at 17.4 percent1. Tokyo Electron's own results presentation, citing Gartner, gives it 23 percent of dry etch and 27 percent of deposition systems in 2025, a category that includes furnace, ALD and low-pressure CVD tools28. Within that category, Tokyo Electron's share of ALD was 15 percent28. The same presentation, again citing Gartner, gives it 91 percent of coater/developers in 202528. These machines coat wafers with photoresist and develop the pattern after lithography.
   - CSET's newer analysis, of data that TechInsights published in July 2026, puts China-based suppliers at 9.8 percent of deposition in 20252. They held 10.1 percent of etch and clean that year12. In dry etch alone their share was 12.4 percent1. In ALD they held 2.6 percent in 2025, most of it from Piotech6.
   - The Chinese toolmaker AMEC alone held 12.4 percent of dielectric etch in 2025, by CSET's count from TechInsights data5. Its revenue was RMB 12.385 billion that year, up about 36.62 percent36. It spent RMB 3.744 billion on research and development, 30.2 percent of sales36.
   - October 2023. A rule issued in October 2023 and effective 17 November rewrote the US control list's entries for chipmaking equipment40. It added tools for four processes7. The first is dry etch, both sideways (isotropic) and vertical (anisotropic), under ECCN 3B001.c.123. The second is spatial ALD (3B001.d.9), whose design keeps stray particles down to the level advanced chips need7. The third is low-fluorine tungsten ALD and CVD (3B001.d.10), which fills deep, narrow gaps in advanced chips with tungsten while keeping resistance low7. The fourth is carbon hard mask PECVD (3B001.d.5), which lays down the etch-resistant carbon films that smaller features need7.
   - December 2024. BIS controlled 24 more types of equipment and three types of software, and added 140 entities to the Entity List9. The new equipment entries include TSV etch, and deposition into features with aspect ratios above 200:141. BIS also wrote a foreign direct product rule that reaches some tools built outside the United States9. The rule largely exempts foreign-made tools exported by firms located in Japan, the Netherlands and the 31 other countries it lists, whose governments can control these tools themselves42. Japan, one of those countries, had added advanced chipmaking equipment to its own control list on 23 July 202343.
   - Source CSET analysis of TechInsights data (published July 2026), ETO Chip Explorer 2
   - Deposition and etch tools are a chokepoint, but a weaker one than lithography, because chipmakers can buy each main tool from more than one maker. In dry etch, Lam Research held 41.1 percent in 2025, Tokyo Electron 23.4 percent and Applied Materials 17.4 percent, by CSET's count1. Atomic layer deposition has at least five makers6.

   - Eight firms besides KLA each held more than 1 percent of the market in 20251. But KLA alone held 85.9 percent of defect inspection, the largest kind, that year1.
   - Chinese firms held 0.7 percent of the world market for tools that measure and inspect wafers in 2025, by CSET's count1. Skyverse, a Shenzhen maker of inspection and metrology tools, reported 2,053,298,171.74 yuan of revenue in 2025, up 48.75 percent on 20243.
   - In 2025 the American firm KLA was the world's largest maker of metrology and inspection tools1. Citing Gartner, KLA puts its 2024 share at 56.5 percent, 6.5 times its nearest rival's share4.
   - Metrology (measurement) and inspection are a chip factory's quality control. Between the steps that build a chip, another machine looks the wafer over. It checks whether the lines are the right width, whether this layer landed squarely on the last one, and whether a speck of dirt has killed a circuit. The main chip in an AI accelerator, the one that does the calculating, is one of the largest cut from a wafer. A bigger chip is a bigger target for a speck. In a textbook example with the same density of specks, 49.2 percent of chips 1 centimeter on a side come out flawless, but only 11.3 percent of chips 2 centimeters on a side5. In 2025 one American firm, KLA, held 56.8 percent of the market for these tools, by CSET's count from TechInsights data1. So a chip factory, or fab, cut off from KLA would find fewer of the faults that make its chips fail.
   - By CSET's count from TechInsights data, defect inspection was the largest kind of measuring and inspection tool in 2025, a $6.7 billion market1. KLA held 85.9 percent of it1. Chinese makers held 0.7 percent of the whole market that year by the same count1. Yet China took 26 percent of KLA's revenue in the June 2026 quarter6.
   - Since October 2022 the US has required a license for any tool subject to US export rules bound for Chinese fabs that make advanced logic or memory chips7. A December 2024 rule added specific tools to the export control list, among them inspection tools that can find defects of 21 nm or smaller8.
   - Process control tools, the machines that measure and inspect wafers and masks, were a $15.7 billion market in 2025, by CSET's count from TechInsights data1. CSET is a research center at Georgetown University. KLA puts the whole wafer equipment market, including advanced packaging, at about $120 billion that year2.
   - KLA held more than half of this market in 2025, several times the share of any rival. CSET's count puts KLA at 56.8 percent and Applied Materials, the next largest, at 11.2 percent1. At its March 2026 investor day KLA cited Gartner's figures for 2024, which put its share at 56.5 percent, 6.5 times its nearest competitor's4. In April 2026 KLA said its 2025 share was about 7 times the nearest competitor's9.
   - A bigger chip is a bigger target for the second kind, so the cost of each good chip grows faster than its area5. Chipmakers call each chip on a wafer a die. In the standard formula, die yield is (1 + D0A/a) to the power minus a. Here D0 is defects per square centimeter, A is die area, and a is a clustering constant of 3 to 4 that says how much defects bunch together. At a defect density of 0.8 per square centimeter, with a set to 3, a 1 square centimeter die yields 49.2 percent and a 2 by 2 centimeter die 11.3 percent5. Because the bigger die yields less and fits fewer times on a wafer, it costs 20.1 times as much as the small one5.
   - All the market sizes and shares below are CSET's, for 2025, from TechInsights data1.
   - Source ETO Supply Chain Explorer, CSET 1
   - Source ETO Supply Chain Explorer, CSET 1
   - KLA says its share grew 3.6 percentage points from 2021 to 20259. It says it gained share again in 2025 across mask, optical wafer and e-beam inspection9. Its revenue for fiscal 2026, the year to June 2026, was $13.579 billion, up from $12.156 billion in fiscal 202518.
   - KLA's rivals are far smaller, and none holds more than 11.2 percent, by CSET's count from TechInsights data for 20251. Eight of them each held more than 1 percent of the market that year1.
   - Lasertec holds 6.4 percent, almost all of it mask inspection1. Hitachi High-Tech holds 6.2 percent, most of it from CD-SEMs1. ASML holds 5.9 percent through YieldStar overlay and HMI e-beam metrology1.
   - Nova holds 4.3 percent, through film metrology1. It took $880.6 million of revenue in 202519.
   - Onto Innovation holds 2.6 percent1. It took $1,005.3 million of revenue in 202520.
   - Camtek holds 1.6 percent1. It took $496.1 million of revenue in 202521.
   - KLA's patterning line, which includes several metrology products and mask inspection, grew 61 percent year on year in the June 2026 quarter6.
   - Source KLA letter to shareholders, Q4 FY2026 6
   - KLA says the AI build-out is making advanced packaging, and the process control it needs, more important24. KLA says it became the largest supplier of process control tools for advanced wafer-level packaging in 2025, adding 14 percentage points of share9. It expects about $1.1 billion of packaging process control revenue in 2026, up more than 70 percent24. In February 2026 Onto said it had signed a volume purchase agreement estimated at over $240 million with a high-bandwidth memory (HBM) maker20. The deal covers 2D inspection and 3D bump metrology, which measures the tiny metal bumps that join stacked chips, through 202720.
   - Chinese makers are still small in process control, but one of them is growing fast. CSET's count, built on TechInsights data, puts all Chinese firms at 0.7 percent of the market in 20251. Skyverse (Shenzhen Zhongke Feice), a maker of wafer inspection and metrology tools, reported 2,053,298,171.74 yuan of revenue in 20253. That was up 48.75 percent on 20243. In its 2025 annual report Skyverse says Chinese makers still hold a low share of China's market for these tools and that KLA holds the largest share of it25. In the June 2026 quarter China took 26 percent of KLA's revenue and Taiwan 31 percent6.
   - In October 2022 the US began requiring a license for any item subject to US export rules, process control tools included, bound for certain Chinese fabs7. The rule covered fabs that made logic chips at 16/14 nm or below, NAND memory with 128 or more layers, or DRAM at 18 nm half-pitch or below31.
   - A fab that can buy scanners and etchers but not sensitive inspection tools finds fewer of the defects that lower its yield, so its yield rises more slowly. Most of those tools come from one firm, KLA, which held more than half the process control market in 20251. KLA led four kinds of process control tool that year, by CSET's count1. It held 85.9 percent of defect inspection, the largest kind1. Still, every kind of process control tool had a second maker that year, by CSET's count from TechInsights data1. KLA says its share of the whole market grew from 2021 to 20259.

== #transistors-and-front-end: 296 lines before, 299 after; as printed only before 26, only after 29; marks out only before 1, only after 4; card 49 lines, the same words; order with the card set aside the same
   sources: 70 lines before, 70 after, the same set, in the new reading order
   the card before: after "A gate-all-around transistor, the switch in a 2 nm-class chip, drawn a", before "In plain terms"
   the card after:  after "Nvidia's Blackwell GPU holds 208 billion switches on two dies, each di", before "How it works#"
   words only before: Extreme
   words only after:  Extreme concentration
   words only after:  ·
   words only after:  Hard to substitute
   words only after:  Chokepoint card
   - Extreme
   - TSMC, Intel and Samsung all make chips with gate-all-around transistors, the newest kind1.
   - per 300 mm wafer on TSMC's 4NP process, Epoch AI's December 2025 estimate2
   - N2 in volume production since 4Q25, TSMC's first nanosheet node3
   - 18A in high-volume production in the United States, as of 20264
   - Started initial production of its 3 nm gate-all-around process in June 20225
   - N+3, a 7 nm-class process made without EUV, SemiAnalysis found in June 20266
   - Said in July 2025 it would begin 2 nm mass production in 20277
   - Rapidus, a newcomer, is building on IBM's 2 nm technology under a December 2022 agreement8. Since September 2024 the United States has required a license to share the know-how for building these transistors, except with countries that impose matching controls9.
   - SMIC makes chips on its N+3 process, which SemiAnalysis measured in June 2026 at 113.4 million transistors per square millimeter, against 107.7 million for TSMC's N66. It builds its tightest layers in several patterning steps because it cannot buy an extreme-ultraviolet scanner6.
   - In August 2025 Intel called itself the only chipmaker that both develops and manufactures leading-edge logic chips in the United States10. It says its 18A process brought gate-all-around transistors and backside power to market together in 202511.
   - Intel's 18A process shipped gate-all-around transistors together with backside power, a second power network built underneath them11. TSMC says its A14 process will give up to 15 percent more speed or up to 30 percent less power than N212. Rapidus, a state-backed newcomer in Japan, said in July 2025 that it would begin 2 nm mass production in 20277.
   - China's SMIC makes 7 nm-class chips without extreme-ultraviolet (EUV) lithography, the most advanced way to print chip patterns6. China has announced $43 billion to develop its own EUV13. In September 2025 the Center for Strategic and International Studies judged that EUV remains firmly out of reach13.
   - Nvidia's Blackwell GPU holds 208 billion switches on two dies, each die the largest a lithography machine can print in one shot14. Every switch has to turn on fully, let almost no current through when off, and keep doing it for years. Making the switches is called the front end of line. Wiring them together is the back end of line.
   - TSMC's N2, its first nanosheet node, entered volume production in the fourth quarter of 20253. TSMC rates the follow-on N2P at 18 percent more speed at the same power and 36 percent less power at the same speed than its earlier N3E21. It gives N2P 1.2 times the logic density of N3E and 1.15 times its chip density, but its technology page does not define either measure21.
   - Intel and Samsung also make gate-all-around transistors. Intel's version, called RibbonFET, is built into its 18A process, which Intel says is in high-volume production in the United States4. An improved version, 18A-P, adds another 9 percent performance at the same power11. Samsung got there first, starting initial production of its 3 nm gate-all-around process in June 20225. But SemiAnalysis reported in February 2023 that, because of yield problems, high-volume chips on Samsung's 3 nm gate-all-around technology were expected only in 202422.
   - Intel's version, PowerVia, is part of 18A, and Intel calls it an industry first4. It cut routed area, the space a block of circuits takes once its wiring is laid out, by 11 percent and dynamic voltage droop tenfold11. Droop is the sag in supply voltage when a block of logic switches on at once. TSMC's Super Power Rail connects the backside network directly to each transistor's source and drain, the two ends of its channel23. Against N2P, TSMC rates A16, which uses Super Power Rail, at 8 to 10 percent more speed, 15 to 20 percent less power and up to 1.10 times the chip density21.
   - TSMC plans to start A14 production in 2028, with more than 20 percent higher logic density than N212. It said in April 2026 that two more processes, A13 and A12, are scheduled to enter production in 202924. A13 is a direct shrink of A14 with 6 percent area savings, and A12 adds Super Power Rail to the A14 platform24.
   - TSMC, Intel and Samsung build gate-all-around nodes with extreme-ultraviolet (EUV) lithography. China's SMIC builds 7 nm-class nodes without it6.
   - SemiAnalysis reported in September 2026 that Intel's Panther Lake, Samsung's Exynos 2600 and Apple's A20 Pro, which is made on TSMC's N2, all use gate-all-around transistors1. In August 2025 Intel called itself the only semiconductor company that both develops and manufactures leading-edge logic chips in the United States10.
   - In July 2025 Rapidus, a state-backed newcomer in Japan, said it had started making 2 nm prototypes at its plant in Chitose, Hokkaido, and would begin mass production in 20277. Its process builds on IBM's 2 nm technology, which the two firms agreed in December 2022 to develop further for production at Rapidus's plant in Japan8. By June 2026 Rapidus had raised 424.95 billion yen in equity29. The most recent 150 billion yen came from the Information-Technology Promotion Agency, a state agency under Japan's industry ministry29. It has also received government research subsidies since fiscal 202229.
   - A node's name, such as 7 nm, does not measure any feature on the chip. Applied Materials, a toolmaker, said in 2022 that chipmakers raise density in two ways, by printing smaller features and by laying out each logic cell more tightly18. SMIC's N+3, its third-generation 7 nm process, sets its narrowest wires 32.5 nm apart, tighter than the 36 nm in Intel's Panther Lake chips on 18A6. But the 18A process itself allows wires 32 nm apart6. SemiAnalysis called the comparison an incomplete, cherry-picked metric6. N+3 packs 113.4 million transistors per square millimeter, against 107.7 for TSMC's N66.
   - Nvidia's Blackwell is made on an older TSMC process than AMD's MI455X. Blackwell uses a custom TSMC 4NP process14. Epoch AI estimated in December 2025 that a 300 mm wafer on that process costs $17,0002. AMD's MI455X, launched on 23 July 2026, is made on TSMC's 2 nm and 3 nm processes31. It spreads its compute across eight separate dies32.
   - TSMC, Intel and Samsung do not sell their leading-edge process flows, which set the steps, their order and the tools for each. In September 2024 the United States began requiring a license to share the know-how for building gate-all-around transistors, except with countries that impose matching controls9.
   - China has money for chipmaking but cannot buy EUV scanners. It launched its third National Integrated Circuit Fund in May 2024 with 344 billion yuan, about $47 billion34. China's 7 nm chips have been made on deep-ultraviolet machines, a generation behind EUV13. China had stockpiled those machines from ASML, the Dutch scanner maker, before export restrictions took effect13. SMIC gets fine features out of them by building its tightest layers in several patterning steps6.
   - Domestic EUV is further off. China has announced $43 billion to develop it13. In September 2025 the Center for Strategic and International Studies (CSIS) judged that EUV remains firmly out of reach despite massive state investment13.

   + Hard to substitute

   - Over $37bn planned in Texas (CHIPS award, Dec 2024)5
   - $9.327bn revenue in 20256
   - Raised 267.6 billion yen in February 2026 to reach 2 nm mass production by 20277
   - In March 2026 the Semiconductor Industry Association (SIA) put the cost to build and equip a leading-edge fab at as much as $20 to $25 billion8. SemiAnalysis judged in December 2025 that finding the highly skilled engineers a foundry needs remained a major challenge for TSMC in Arizona9.
   - Georgetown University's CSET reported in June 2024 that Huawei's Ascend 910B AI chip is believed to be made on SMIC's 7 nm-class N+2 process10. But SemiAnalysis judged in April 2025 that most 910B and 910C chips were made at TSMC11.
   - The US government agreed in August 2025 to buy a passive 9.9 percent stake in Intel, with no board seat12. Announcing the deal, Intel called itself the only chipmaker that both develops and manufactures leading-edge logic chips in the United States12. Intel Foundry lost $2.089 billion in the second quarter of 202613.
   - In 2022 Taiwan held 69 percent of the world's capacity for advanced logic chips, those below 10 nanometers, and the United States almost none14. In July 2026 TSMC raised its planned investment in Arizona to $265 billion across 12 facilities15.
   - The industry association SEMI projected in 2026 that China would keep the world's largest installed fab capacity in 2026 and 202716. China's SMIC cannot buy an EUV scanner, the machine that prints the finest layers, yet it makes 7 nm-class chips by printing those layers in several passes on older deep-ultraviolet scanners17.
   - TSMC calls a plant a GIGAFAB once it can run more than 100,000 wafers a month, each a 300 mm disc, twelve inches across18. It operates six of them, and every one is in Taiwan1.
   - A new leading-edge fab costs tens of billions of dollars. In 2023 the Boston Consulting Group (BCG) estimated that a new fab for chips at 2 nm and below, completed in 2026, would cost $35 to $43 billion to build, equip and run over ten years2. BCG said that was 33 to 66 percent more than the same cost in 20232. Building and equipping alone takes as much as $20 to $25 billion of capital for a leading-edge fab, the Semiconductor Industry Association (SIA) told the US Senate in March 20268.
   - Most of TSMC's capacity is in its six largest plants, all of them in Taiwan. Together, the plants TSMC and its subsidiaries run had capacity for more than 17 million wafers a year in 2025, counted as if each were the standard 12-inch size1. The six largest plants had a combined capacity of more than 13 million18. The other plants are five smaller fabs in Taiwan, two in China, two in the United States and one in Japan1.
   - TSMC and UMC, both based in Taiwan, make chips only for other companies. TSMC, founded in 1987, pioneered this pure-play model23. Chips at 7 nm and below, meaning the newest processes, were 79 percent of TSMC's wafer revenue in the third quarter of 20263. That share split into 33 percent at 3 nm, 28 at 5 nm, 10 at 7 nm and 8 at 2 nm3. TSMC's revenue for the quarter was $46.72 billion, at a 66.4 percent gross margin3.
   - Source TSMC 3Q26 results, filed with the SEC 3
   - Intel and Samsung design and sell their own chips and rent out capacity too. Renting out capacity is harder for them, for two reasons. Customers dislike handing their designs to a competitor, and each company's own products take its best capacity. Intel Foundry reported $5.8 billion of segment revenue in the second quarter of 2026 and an operating loss of $2.089 billion13. Only $293 million of that revenue came from outside customers4. Intel says substantially all of its foundry business still makes Intel's own products4.
   - China's SMIC and Hua Hong and Japan's Rapidus have government backing. SMIC cannot buy an extreme ultraviolet (EUV) scanner, the machine that prints the finest layers17. Yet it makes 7 nm-class chips on its N+2 and N+3 processes by printing those layers in several passes on older deep-ultraviolet scanners17. SMIC's revenue rose 16.2 percent to $9.327 billion in 2025, at a 21 percent gross margin6. Its lines ran 93.5 percent full, on monthly capacity above a million wafers counted in the smaller 8-inch size6.
   - Two research groups disagree about where Huawei's Ascend AI chips are made. Georgetown University's Center for Security and Emerging Technology (CSET) reported in June 2024 that Huawei's Ascend 910B AI chip was believed to be made on SMIC's N+2 process10. It also reported that SMIC's limited 7 nm capacity forced Huawei to choose between its most advanced phone chips and its AI chips10. But the research firm SemiAnalysis judged in April 2025 that most Ascend 910B and 910C chips were made on TSMC's 7 nm process11. It said Huawei got around the sanctions that barred it from TSMC by buying about $500 million of 7 nm wafers through another company, Sophgo11.
   - In February 2026 Rapidus raised 267.6 billion yen from the Japanese government and 32 companies to reach 2 nm mass production by 20277.
   - In March 2025 TSMC announced plans to add $100 billion to the $65 billion it was already investing in Phoenix, for three more fabs, two packaging plants and an R&D center27. In July 2026 it announced another $100 billion for four more fabs at 2 nm or below, taking its planned investment in Arizona to $265 billion across 12 facilities15.
   - In August 2025 the US government agreed to buy 433.3 million newly issued Intel shares at $20.47 each, or $8.9 billion in all, for a 9.9 percent stake12. It would pay with $5.7 billion of CHIPS Act grants that Intel had not yet received and $3.2 billion awarded to Intel under the Secure Enclave program12. Intel said it would keep meeting its Secure Enclave obligations and delivering trusted and secure chips to the US Department of Defense12. Announcing the deal, Intel's chief executive, Lip-Bu Tan, called Intel the only semiconductor company that both develops and manufactures leading-edge logic chips in the United States12. The deal closed on 27 August 2025 and made the government one of Intel's largest stockholders, Intel said in its annual report for 202530.
   - The stake is passive, with no board seat12. The government agreed to vote with Intel's board on matters that need shareholder approval, with limited exceptions12. The deal also gave the government a five-year warrant, a right to buy a further 5 percent of Intel at $20 a share12. The government can use that right only if Intel's ownership of its foundry business falls below 51 percent12.
   - In 2026 Intel began high-volume manufacturing of some of its Panther Lake processors using ASML's High-NA extreme ultraviolet scanners13. It also put its 18A-P process into risk production, the trial runs a process goes through before it is signed off13.
   - In 2022 Samsung broke ground in Taylor, Texas, on a plant with an initial minimum investment of $17 billion, split into $6 billion for buildings and $11 billion for equipment31. In December 2024 the US Commerce Department awarded Samsung up to $4.745 billion of CHIPS Act funding, to support more than $37 billion of planned investment in Central Texas5. That plan covers two new leading-edge logic fabs and a research fab in Taylor, and an expansion of Samsung's existing plant in Austin5.
   - In 2022 South Korea and Taiwan held almost all of the world's logic capacity below 10 nm32. The United States had almost no capacity below 10 nm that year32. In May 2024 SIA and BCG projected that the US share of that capacity would reach 28 percent by 203214. They also projected that new fab investment worldwide would reach around $2.3 trillion from 2024 to 203233.
   - China leads the world in capacity for logic chips made at 28 nm or older, but little of its capacity is advanced34. Across all nodes, old and new, SEMI forecast in June 2024 that China would have installed capacity of 10.1 million wafers a month in 2025, the most of any region25. It forecast 5.8 million for Taiwan, out of a world total of 33.7 million, all counted in the 8-inch size25. In the third-quarter 2026 update of its World Fab Forecast, SEMI projected that China would keep the largest installed capacity in 2026 and 202716.
   - Political. Taiwan held 69 percent of the world's logic capacity below 10 nm in 202214. In December 2025 SemiAnalysis put Taiwan's share of the world's advanced-node capacity at over 90 percent, though it did not say which node sizes count as advanced9. So a blockade or conflict would cut most of that output off from the world market at once. See Who Controls the Chain.
   - Building capacity outside Taiwan is slow, and TSMC has yet to build a gigafab anywhere else1. SemiAnalysis wrote in December 2025 that a foundry needs highly skilled engineers to get the most working chips from each wafer9. It judged that finding enough workers remained a major challenge for TSMC in Arizona9.

== #memory-and-hbm: 284 lines before, 287 after; as printed only before 12, only after 15; marks out only before 1, only after 4; card 43 lines, the same words; order with the card set aside the same
   sources: 48 lines before, 48 after, the same set, in the new reading order
   the card before: after "A twelve-high HBM stack pulled apart: a logic die at the base, shown i", before "In plain terms"
   the card after:  after "An AI accelerator moves numbers from memory to its arithmetic units an", before "How it works#"
   words only before: Extreme
   words only after:  Extreme concentration
   words only after:  ·
   words only after:  Hard to substitute
   words only after:  Chokepoint card
   - Extreme
   - HBM market in 2025, Micron's December 2025 estimate2
   - The Institute for Progress estimated in October 2025 that China would make virtually no HBM that year3. Huawei said in September 2025 that its Ascend 950 chips would use two HBM designs of its own, without saying who would manufacture the memory4.
   - Micron is the only American HBM maker1. As of October 2026, License Exception HBM lets US and allied exporters ship HBM below 3.3 GB per second per square millimeter without a license, including to China when the chip designer buys it directly and it goes straight to the packaging site5.
   - Since December 2024, a US license has been required, unless an exception applies, to export to China any HBM moving more than 2 GB per second per square millimeter of its area6. The Institute for Progress reported in October 2025 that China's CXMT planned to produce HBM3 in 20263. Even so, working from TrendForce's estimate of world HBM shipments, the institute projected that producers outside China would make 70 times as much HBM as China in 20263. In June 2026 SemiAnalysis judged that CXMT was still struggling to stabilize supply of eight-high HBM37.
   - An AI accelerator moves numbers from memory to its arithmetic units and back. The US export control office wrote in December 2024 that a processor cannot reach its full capabilities unless its memory capacity and bandwidth rise with its speed6.
   - Source Micron prepared remarks, fiscal first quarter 2026, 17 December 2025 2
   - As HBM grows, memory makers have less factory capacity left for traditional DRAM. In December 2025 Micron put the HBM market at about $35 billion in 2025 and forecast that it would reach around $100 billion in 20282. Micron says HBM needs more wafers than traditional DRAM, the kind used in PCs and phones, to make the same number of bits14. SK hynix says the industry's shift of production capacity to HBM has left traditional DRAM undersupplied1. The shortage, it says, helped drive a significant price recovery for that DRAM from the third quarter of 20251.
   - A US export rule of December 2024 covers every HBM stack that was in production when it was published. The Bureau of Industry and Security (BIS), the US export control office, published the rule on 5 December 20246. It added an export classification, ECCN 3A090.c, for "High bandwidth memory (HBM) having a 'memory bandwidth density' greater than 2 gigabytes per second per square millimeter"6. Its technical note defines that density as the memory bandwidth in gigabytes per second divided by the area of the package or stack in square millimeters6. The rule also covers stacks made outside the United States, through a foreign direct product rule, which applies US controls to goods made abroad with American technology6. BIS stated in the rule that all HBM stacks then in production exceeded that threshold6.
   - Two provisions narrow the control. License Exception HBM, at 15 CFR 740.25, applies to exporters headquartered in the United States or a Country Group A:5 ally5. It lets them ship HBM below 3.3 GB per second per square millimeter without a license, including to Macau and Country Group D:5 destinations such as China5. HBM sent to those destinations must be bought directly by the designer of the chip it will be packaged into5. Every shipment under the exception must go straight to the packaging site5. The rule also excludes co-packaged chips that combine HBM and logic and whose main function is processing, which other chip controls may cover instead6.
   - China's strongest DRAM maker, CXMT, was still struggling to make HBM in steady supply by June 2026. In December 2024 CSIS called CXMT one of only two Chinese firms with a credible path to producing advanced HBM, though it did not name the other24. The Institute for Progress reported in October 2025 that CXMT was planning to produce HBM3 in 20263. It also estimated that China would make virtually no HBM in 20253. In June 2026 SemiAnalysis judged that CXMT was still struggling to stabilize supply of eight-high HBM3, with even greater challenges at twelve-high7.
   - Huawei plans to use HBM of its own design in its Ascend 950 AI chips, but when it announced the designs in September 2025 it did not say who would make the memory4. The two designs are called HiBL 1.0 and HiZQ 2.04. Huawei said the 950DT, due in the fourth quarter of 2026, would carry 144 GB of HiZQ 2.0 at 4 TB/s4.
   + Extreme concentration
   + ·
   + Hard to substitute

== #advanced-packaging: 336 lines before, 339 after; as printed only before 36, only after 39; marks out only before 1, only after 4; card 52 lines, the same words; order with the card set aside the same
   sources: 66 lines before, 66 after, the same set, in the new reading order
   the card before: after "A CoWoS-L package, pulled apart. Two logic dies and eight HBM stacks s", before "In plain terms"
   the card after:  after "A lithography scanner prints a rectangle 26 mm by 33 mm, or 858 mm2, i", before "How it works#"
   words only before: Extreme
   words only after:  Extreme concentration
   words only after:  ·
   words only after:  Moderately hard to substitute
   words only after:  Chokepoint card
   - Extreme
   - Rated on CoWoS capacity, which TSMC said in July 2026 was so tight that it limited its customers' growth1.
   - for the CoWoS-L packaging of one Nvidia B200, against about $900 for its two logic dies, by Epoch AI's December 2025 estimate2
   - CoWoS packaging used by Nvidia, Google, AMD and Amazon in 20253
   - Foveros 3D packaging in volume at Rio Rancho, New Mexico, since January 20244
   - NT$389.2B assembly, test and materials revenue, 20255
   - $6.71B net sales, 20256
   - RMB 38.87B revenue, 20257
   - Thermocompression bonders, with $532.1M of advanced packaging revenue in 20258
   - CoWoS packaging has been outsourced to assembly firms such as ASE and Amkor before9. Intel's EMIB, which links chips through silicon bridges embedded in the substrate, is another option, and Amazon's Trainium and Google's TPU were both adopting it as of March 20269. But a newcomer would have to match TSMC, which said in April 2026 that it was producing CoWoS at 5.5 times the area a scanner prints in one shot10.
   - Chinese firms had 5.3 percent of the market for fabrication tools built for advanced packaging in 2025, by CSET's analysis of TechInsights data11. Between 2019 and 2024 they gained 5 percentage points of share in test tools but lost share in bonding and other conventional assembly and packaging tools12.
   - Intel opened Fab 9 in Rio Rancho, New Mexico, in January 2024 to make its Foveros 3D packages in volume4. Amkor broke ground in October 2025 on a $7B packaging and test campus in Peoria, Arizona, and expects production to start in early 202813.
   - Epoch AI estimates that in 2025 Nvidia used 60.3 percent of all capacity for CoWoS, TSMC's interposer packaging3. On the July 2026 earnings call, TSMC's chief executive, C.C. Wei, said its packaging capacity was so tight that it limited customers' growth1. Asked about Intel's EMIB-T packaging, he said he hoped it would take some of the load off TSMC1.
   - In a TrendForce ranking by 2024 revenue, which CSET cites, four of the top ten outsourced firms that package and test chips are headquartered in China12. But in 2025 Chinese firms had 5.3 percent of the market for advanced packaging fabrication tools, against 65.8 percent for US firms, by CSET's analysis of TechInsights data11.
   - A lithography scanner prints a rectangle 26 mm by 33 mm, or 858 mm2, in one exposure14. That area is called the reticle field. Every leading AI accelerator, counting its memory stacks, covers more area than that. Packaging is the stage that lets an accelerator be larger than one reticle field, and the packaging method limits how much larger it can be.
   - A second reason for the interposer is high bandwidth memory (HBM). An HBM4 stack connects through 2,048 data signals, packed too tightly for a standard resin substrate, so the memory sits beside the logic on the interposer15. Even Nvidia's H100, whose single logic die is 814 mm2 and fits inside one reticle field, carries five HBM stacks16. Epoch AI counts TSMC's CoWoS packaging among the three inputs the H100 needs, along with its logic die and its HBM3.
   - One cracked joint can scrap a package holding eight tested HBM stacks and two large, expensive logic dies. Epoch AI estimated in December 2025 that CoWoS-L packaging, the version built with small silicon bridges, added about $1,100 to the cost of building one Nvidia B2002. That is more than its estimate of about $900 for the B200's two logic dies2.
   - Intel's EMIB embeds similar silicon bridges in the resin substrate17. SemiAnalysis reported in March 2026 that EMIB was gaining traction, with Amazon's Trainium and Google's TPU both adopting it9.
   - TSMC put SoIC into volume production in 2022, stacking SRAM memory chips on logic wafers23. Its 3 nm SoIC entered volume production in 202519. An A14-on-A14 version, stacking dies made on TSMC's 1.4 nm process, is due in 202910. It will carry 1.8 times the die-to-die connection density of SoIC that stacks 2 nm dies on 2 nm dies10.
   - SoIC bonding also brings optics into the package. TSMC's COUPE uses SoIC to join a silicon photonics chip, which sends data as light, to its electrical control chip19. TSMC said in April 2026 that COUPE on substrate would begin production in 202610. It also said that putting COUPE inside the package gives twice the power efficiency of a pluggable version on the circuit board10.
   - TSMC does not publish its CoWoS capacity3. Industry estimates from SemiWiki and TrendForce, compiled by Epoch AI, put it at about 65,000 to 75,000 wafers a month at the end of 2025, up from 35,000 to 40,000 a year earlier3.
   - By Epoch AI's estimate, four chip designers used most of the world's CoWoS capacity in 2025 but only a small share of its advanced chipmaking. Epoch AI compared each chip designer's use of components with world supply3. It puts Nvidia at 60.3 percent of all CoWoS capacity in 2025, with a 90 percent confidence interval of 56.5 to 64.33. Google took 13.5 percent, AMD 8.4 and Amazon 7.43. Together those four used around 90 percent of global CoWoS capacity and HBM supply, with HBM measured by value3. But they used only 12 percent of advanced logic die production3.
   - Source Epoch AI, advanced packaging and HBM were the bottlenecks on AI chip production in 2025 3
   - In March 2026 the research firm SemiAnalysis judged that front-end capacity had become the dominant bottleneck and that CoWoS constraints were easing9. It said TSMC was planning CoWoS capacity around how many wafers N3, its 3 nm process, could produce, because added packaging capacity is of no use without front-end wafers to fill it9. It also noted that CoWoS packaging can be, and has been, outsourced to assembly firms such as ASE and Amkor9.
   - TSMC gave a different account four months later. On the July 2026 earnings call, Wei said TSMC's packaging capacity was so tight that it limited customers' growth1. Asked about Intel's EMIB-T, he said he welcomed the extra flexibility and hoped it would take some of the load off TSMC1. Later in the call he said back-end capacity was still in shortage26.
   - Source TSMC 2025 annual report and 2026 technology symposium 10
   - Three kinds of firm package chips. TSMC, which makes chips for others, and Intel, which makes its own, do it in their own back-end fabs. Outsourced assembly and test firms do the rest. Intel opened Fab 9 in Rio Rancho, New Mexico, in January 2024 to make its Foveros 3D packages in volume4. It called the plant the only US factory producing the most advanced packaging at scale4.
   - ASE leads the outsourced assembly and test firms, and Amkor is the largest one headquartered in the United States. ASE Technology Holding had revenue of NT$645.4 billion in 2025, about 40 percent of it from its electronics manufacturing arm, a business separate from assembly and test5. Its assembly, test and materials business brought in NT$389.2 billion, up from NT$325.9 billion in 20245. Amkor had net sales of $6.71 billion in 20256.
   - Thermocompression bonding. Pressing dies together under heat and force. ASMPT's advanced packaging revenue reached $532.1 million in 2025, with thermocompression bonder revenue up about 146 percent8. ASMPT targets 35 to 40 percent of a market it puts at $1.6 billion in 20288.
   - TSMC and Amkor both plan advanced packaging plants in Arizona. TSMC's Arizona commitment reached $265 billion in July 2026 and covers two advanced packaging plants alongside 10 fabs29. Amkor started building a $7 billion campus in Peoria, Arizona, in October 2025 and expects production from early 202813. The US Commerce Department awarded Amkor up to $407 million in CHIPS Act funding for the site in December 202430. In October 2025 Wei said a large outsourced packaging partner, which he did not name, was already breaking ground in Arizona31. He said its plant was on an earlier schedule than TSMC's own two packaging fabs there31.
   - Source CSET, Inside Beijing’s Chipmaking Offensive 12
   - Four of the top ten outsourced assembly and test firms by 2024 revenue are headquartered in China, by a TrendForce ranking that CSET cites12. JCET, a leading Chinese assembly and test firm, had record revenue of RMB 38.87 billion in 2025, up 8.1 percent7. JCET says its XDFOI packaging platform has entered high-volume manufacturing with redistribution-layer interposer, silicon interposer and silicon bridge options32. Those are the same three kinds of carrier that TSMC sells as CoWoS-R, CoWoS-S and CoWoS-L.
   - Chinese firms had a small share of the market for advanced packaging tools in 2024 and 2025, and they lost ground in bonding. In 2024 Chinese firms had 7 percent of the market for advanced packaging fabrication tools12. Between 2019 and 2024, they gained 5 percentage points of share in test tools and 4 points in fabrication tools built for advanced packaging, by CSET's analysis of TechInsights data12. But they lost share in bonding and other conventional assembly and packaging tools, the category where CSET counts hybrid and thermocompression bonders12. Within the advanced packaging fabrication tools, Chinese firms gained 10 points in etch and clean tools and 3 points in deposition tools12. They lost 2 points each in lithography and polishing (CMP) tools12. CSET's Chip Explorer, a separate data tool also built on TechInsights data, puts Chinese firms at 5.3 percent of the $6.2 billion world market for advanced packaging fabrication tools in 202511. It puts US firms at 65.8 percent and Japanese firms at 15.3 percent11.
   - TSMC said in April 2026 that it was producing CoWoS at 5.5 times the size of a reticle field and planning larger versions10. Each generation on TSMC's roadmap needs a larger interposer.
   - 2028. A 14-reticle interposer, about the area of a compact disc, carrying about ten large compute dies and 20 HBM stacks10.
   - 2029. More than 14 reticles, plus a 40-reticle System-on-Wafer10. TSMC describes System-on-Wafer as joining different data center chips at the scale of a whole wafer33.
   + Extreme concentration
   + ·
   + Moderately hard to substitute
   + Epoch AI estimates that in 2025 Nvidia used 60.3 percent of all capacity for CoWoS, TSMC's interposer packaging1. On the July 2026 earnings call, TSMC's chief executive, C.C. Wei, said its packaging capacity was so tight that it limited customers' growth2. Asked about Intel's EMIB-T packaging, he said he hoped it would take some of the load off TSMC2.
   + In a TrendForce ranking by 2024 revenue, which CSET cites, four of the top ten outsourced firms that package and test chips are headquartered in China3. But in 2025 Chinese firms had 5.3 percent of the market for advanced packaging fabrication tools, against 65.8 percent for US firms, by CSET's analysis of TechInsights data4.
   + Chokepoint card
   + A lithography scanner prints a rectangle 26 mm by 33 mm, or 858 mm2, in one exposure5. That area is called the reticle field. Every leading AI accelerator, counting its memory stacks, covers more area than that. Packaging is the stage that lets an accelerator be larger than one reticle field, and the packaging method limits how much larger it can be.
   + Rated on CoWoS capacity, which TSMC said in July 2026 was so tight that it limited its customers' growth2.
   + for the CoWoS-L packaging of one Nvidia B200, against about $900 for its two logic dies, by Epoch AI's December 2025 estimate6
   + CoWoS packaging used by Nvidia, Google, AMD and Amazon in 20251
   + Foveros 3D packaging in volume at Rio Rancho, New Mexico, since January 20247
   + NT$389.2B assembly, test and materials revenue, 20258
   + $6.71B net sales, 20259
   + RMB 38.87B revenue, 202510
   + Thermocompression bonders, with $532.1M of advanced packaging revenue in 202511
   + CoWoS packaging has been outsourced to assembly firms such as ASE and Amkor before12. Intel's EMIB, which links chips through silicon bridges embedded in the substrate, is another option, and Amazon's Trainium and Google's TPU were both adopting it as of March 202612. But a newcomer would have to match TSMC, which said in April 2026 that it was producing CoWoS at 5.5 times the area a scanner prints in one shot13.
   + Chinese firms had 5.3 percent of the market for fabrication tools built for advanced packaging in 2025, by CSET's analysis of TechInsights data4. Between 2019 and 2024 they gained 5 percentage points of share in test tools but lost share in bonding and other conventional assembly and packaging tools3.
   + Intel opened Fab 9 in Rio Rancho, New Mexico, in January 2024 to make its Foveros 3D packages in volume7. Amkor broke ground in October 2025 on a $7B packaging and test campus in Peoria, Arizona, and expects production to start in early 202814.
   + A second reason for the interposer is high bandwidth memory (HBM). An HBM4 stack connects through 2,048 data signals, packed too tightly for a standard resin substrate, so the memory sits beside the logic on the interposer15. Even Nvidia's H100, whose single logic die is 814 mm2 and fits inside one reticle field, carries five HBM stacks16. Epoch AI counts TSMC's CoWoS packaging among the three inputs the H100 needs, along with its logic die and its HBM1.
   + One cracked joint can scrap a package holding eight tested HBM stacks and two large, expensive logic dies. Epoch AI estimated in December 2025 that CoWoS-L packaging, the version built with small silicon bridges, added about $1,100 to the cost of building one Nvidia B2006. That is more than its estimate of about $900 for the B200's two logic dies6.
   + Intel's EMIB embeds similar silicon bridges in the resin substrate17. SemiAnalysis reported in March 2026 that EMIB was gaining traction, with Amazon's Trainium and Google's TPU both adopting it12.
   + TSMC put SoIC into volume production in 2022, stacking SRAM memory chips on logic wafers23. Its 3 nm SoIC entered volume production in 202519. An A14-on-A14 version, stacking dies made on TSMC's 1.4 nm process, is due in 202913. It will carry 1.8 times the die-to-die connection density of SoIC that stacks 2 nm dies on 2 nm dies13.
   + SoIC bonding also brings optics into the package. TSMC's COUPE uses SoIC to join a silicon photonics chip, which sends data as light, to its electrical control chip19. TSMC said in April 2026 that COUPE on substrate would begin production in 202613. It also said that putting COUPE inside the package gives twice the power efficiency of a pluggable version on the circuit board13.
   + TSMC does not publish its CoWoS capacity1. Industry estimates from SemiWiki and TrendForce, compiled by Epoch AI, put it at about 65,000 to 75,000 wafers a month at the end of 2025, up from 35,000 to 40,000 a year earlier1.
   + By Epoch AI's estimate, four chip designers used most of the world's CoWoS capacity in 2025 but only a small share of its advanced chipmaking. Epoch AI compared each chip designer's use of components with world supply1. It puts Nvidia at 60.3 percent of all CoWoS capacity in 2025, with a 90 percent confidence interval of 56.5 to 64.31. Google took 13.5 percent, AMD 8.4 and Amazon 7.41. Together those four used around 90 percent of global CoWoS capacity and HBM supply, with HBM measured by value1. But they used only 12 percent of advanced logic die production1.

== #substrates-and-pcbs: 329 lines before, 332 after; as printed only before 30, only after 33; marks out only before 1, only after 4; card 49 lines, the same words; order with the card set aside the same
   sources: 94 lines before, 94 after, the same set, in the new reading order
   the card before: after "A build-up substrate, the board a chip sits on, with a quarter cut awa", before "In plain terms"
   the card after:  after "The silicon interposer wires chips together inside a package. Beyond t", before "How it works#"
   words only before: Extreme
   words only after:  Extreme concentration
   words only after:  ·
   words only after:  Hard to substitute
   words only after:  Chokepoint card
   - Extreme
   - Rated on the insulating film inside the substrate, because chipmakers and device makers would have to approve any rival film before substrate makers could use it1.
   - market for FC-BGA substrates in 2025, up 18 percent, in a Prismark estimate cited by AT&S2
   - Told investors in June 2026 that its build-up film has held over 95% of its market since launch3
   - Claimed an overwhelming advantage in low thermal expansion glass in its 2025 integrated report4
   - Said in May 2026 that it typically takes close to 100% of each new generation of interposer-type substrates at launch5
   - Build-up substrates were 52% of sales in Q2 20266
   - Package solution sales KRW 771.6bn in Q2 2026, up 37%7
   - Ajinomoto makes the thin insulating film between the substrate's wiring layers8. Chipmakers and device makers approve substrate materials such as laminate, so a rival film would need their approval too1.
   - TTM, an American circuit board maker, said in its annual report for 2025 that its circuit board competitors are mostly based in China and Taiwan9. Nitto Boseki, a Japanese glass fiber maker, said in its 2025 integrated report that Chinese and Taiwanese companies are aggressively catching up with it in the special glass sector4.
   - The White House's 2021 supply chain review listed no American firm among the key substrate makers10. In December 2024 the US Commerce Department finalized an award of up to $75 million to Absolics, an affiliate of Korea's SKC, for a glass substrate plant in Covington, Georgia11.
   - Ajinomoto's third site for making its build-up film, in Gifu Prefecture, is scheduled to start operating in 2032 to meet demand after 203012. Nitto Boseki says market demand seems to have risen about two to three times since it set its plan for fiscal 2024 to 20274. In January 2026 Nikkei Asia reported that Apple and Qualcomm were among the customers worried about Japanese glass cloth supply13. In August 2026 Ibiden said it had prospects of a third and fourth glass cloth supplier and expected its material procurement risks to decrease14.
   - The silicon interposer wires chips together inside a package. Beyond the interposer, the wiring runs through a build-up substrate made of epoxy and glass, then a printed circuit board that can have more than 30 layers, then a server rack9.
   - One company, Ajinomoto, supplies nearly all the insulating film inside package substrates for logic chips. Ajinomoto Build-up Film, or ABF, is the insulating film between the layers of package substrates for logic chips, used in PCs, network equipment, servers and AI equipment8. Ajinomoto first developed it in 1999 and says major chipmakers have used it as the de facto standard ever since22. On Ajinomoto's own site, its electronic materials division said practically all high-performance computers and servers rely on the film16. In June 2026 Ajinomoto told investors that the film has held a share of over 95 percent since its launch3.
   - Absolics, a subsidiary of Korea's SKC, has a final US Commerce Department award of up to $75 million for a 120,000-square-foot glass substrate plant in Covington, Georgia24. The department finalized the award in December 202411. As of October 2026, the award's project page at NIST, a Commerce Department agency, still said first deliveries to customers were expected in 2025 and production capacity in 2027, without saying whether those deliveries had happened24.
   - Ajinomoto's electronic materials revenue is rising, and it is adding film capacity, but its third production site is not scheduled to open until 2032. Ajinomoto's Healthcare and Others segment, which includes electronic materials such as the film, lifted business profit 45.1 percent to JPY 66.2 billion in the year to March 202627. Ajinomoto attributes the rise mainly to higher electronic materials revenue and higher profit in Bio-Pharma Services & Ingredients27. It has set an investment budget of JPY 25 billion between 2023 and 2030 to expand film production28. It says it has no concerns about its current supply chain overall and will steadily expand its supply capacity over the medium to long term3. In May 2026 it announced the acquisition of land in Gifu Prefecture for a third production site, scheduled to start operating in 2032 to meet demand after 203012.
   - Nitto Boseki says market demand seems to have risen about two to three times since it set its plan for fiscal 2024 to 20274. That plan included about JPY 80 billion of capital spending31. The company claims an overwhelming advantage in quality and capacity for low-expansion glass4. In August 2025 it decided to expand glass fiber cloth production at its Fukushima Enterprise Center, with production scheduled to begin in the fourth quarter of its fiscal 202632. In May 2026 it forecast capital investment of JPY 45,000 million for the year to March 2027, up from JPY 21,702 million in the year to March 202633.
   - In January 2026 Nikkei Asia reported that Apple and Qualcomm were among the customers worried about Japanese glass cloth supply13. In August 2026 Ibiden, a Japanese substrate maker, said it had used only two glass cloth suppliers, in Japan and Taiwan, but now had prospects of a third and fourth for mass production14. Because a Japanese supplier, which Ibiden did not name, also plans to add capacity, Ibiden said it expected its material procurement risks to decrease14.
   - Nitto Boseki says Chinese and Taiwanese companies are aggressively catching up with it in the special glass sector4. In July 2026 Nikkei Asia reported that Chinese chip materials makers were competing with Japanese rivals in the market for chip materials, which the industry association SEMI put at $73.2 billion in 202534.
   - Most of the firms that build substrates for others are Asian. The White House's 2021 hundred-day supply chain review listed build-up substrate among the materials that commenters called vulnerable35. It named key substrate makers including Ibiden, Nanya, Shinko, Samsung, Unimicron, Shennan Circuits, Zhuhai Yueya and AKM, none of them American10.
   - A May 2024 report by the Semiconductor Industry Association and Boston Consulting Group found that every major package substrate vendor was headquartered in Japan, Taiwan or mainland China36. It counted two in Japan, two in Taiwan, one in mainland China and none in the United States36. But TTM Technologies, an American circuit board maker, says it offers package substrates too9.
   - Ibiden will invest about JPY 500 billion in high-performance package substrates from fiscal 2026 to fiscal 2028, starting with about JPY 220 billion, mainly at its Gama plant in Gifu Prefecture37. In August 2026 it said the Gama plant was scheduled to start operating in stages from the end of fiscal 2027 and to add to its results from fiscal 202814. In May 2026 management said Ibiden typically takes close to 100 percent of each new generation of what it calls interposer-type substrates at launch5. It added that this share may decline by 20 to 30 percent after about three to six months5. On silicon-bridge substrates, it said wiring the underside is hard enough that Ibiden expects to keep its technical lead at least through fiscal 20305.
   - Unimicron reported NT$42.9 billion of sales in the second quarter of 20266. By technology, 52 percent came from build-up substrates, and by application, 61 percent came from AI data center work6.
   - Samsung Electro-Mechanics grew package solution sales 37 percent year on year to KRW 771.6 billion in the second quarter of 2026, on substrates for AI accelerators, server processors and cars7.
   - AT&S, an Austrian firm, calls itself a globally leading maker of high-end package substrates38. It makes them in China and Malaysia, and since its 2025/26 financial year also in Austria39. Citing the research firm Prismark, its annual report for 2025/26 valued the market for flip-chip ball grid array (FC-BGA) substrates at $7.5 billion in 2025, up 18 percent from 20242.
   - Source Unimicron 2026 Q2 earnings conference 6
   - TTM Technologies says it regularly builds printed circuit boards of more than 30 layers and can go past 709. Those layers carry power to the GPUs and route fast signals between them, so how much signal the laminate absorbs is a main design constraint. Nitto Boseki also makes NE-glass, a low-dielectric glass cloth that absorbs less signal, used in switches and routers in data centers45.
   - Data center computing was 24 percent of TTM's fiscal 2025 revenue of about $2.9 billion, against 14 percent two years earlier9.
   - Board makers are adding plants outside China and Taiwan, where TTM says most of its circuit board competitors are based9. In its annual report for 2025, TTM said it was building a plant in Syracuse, New York, to make ultra-high-density circuit boards for national security needs9. It expected volume production there in the second half of 20269. Nikkei Asia reported in September 2025 that Thailand aimed to become the biggest circuit board hub outside China as board makers moved to diversify production46.
   - The few firms that build substrates are spending to add capacity, but it takes years. In August 2026 Ibiden said that, with supply short across the industry, the average price of package substrates had risen more than it expected14. The materials inside the substrate are harder to replace than the firms that build it. Mitsubishi Gas Chemical, a laminate maker, says chipmakers and electronic device makers further down the chain approve the materials it sells to substrate makers1. So a rival to Ajinomoto's film or to Nitto Boseki's low-expansion glass cloth would first need approval from those chipmakers and device makers.

   + Nitto Boseki says Chinese and Taiwanese companies are aggressively catching up with it in the special glass sector2. In July 2026 Nikkei Asia reported that Chinese chip materials makers were competing with Japanese rivals in the market for chip materials, which the industry association SEMI put at $73.2 billion in 202534.
   + Most of the firms that build substrates for others are Asian. The White House's 2021 hundred-day supply chain review listed build-up substrate among the materials that commenters called vulnerable35. It named key substrate makers including Ibiden, Nanya, Shinko, Samsung, Unimicron, Shennan Circuits, Zhuhai Yueya and AKM, none of them American13.
   + A May 2024 report by the Semiconductor Industry Association and Boston Consulting Group found that every major package substrate vendor was headquartered in Japan, Taiwan or mainland China36. It counted two in Japan, two in Taiwan, one in mainland China and none in the United States36. But TTM Technologies, an American circuit board maker, says it offers package substrates too5.
   + Ibiden will invest about JPY 500 billion in high-performance package substrates from fiscal 2026 to fiscal 2028, starting with about JPY 220 billion, mainly at its Gama plant in Gifu Prefecture37. In August 2026 it said the Gama plant was scheduled to start operating in stages from the end of fiscal 2027 and to add to its results from fiscal 20284. In May 2026 management said Ibiden typically takes close to 100 percent of each new generation of what it calls interposer-type substrates at launch9. It added that this share may decline by 20 to 30 percent after about three to six months9. On silicon-bridge substrates, it said wiring the underside is hard enough that Ibiden expects to keep its technical lead at least through fiscal 20309.
   + Unimicron reported NT$42.9 billion of sales in the second quarter of 202610. By technology, 52 percent came from build-up substrates, and by application, 61 percent came from AI data center work10.
   + Samsung Electro-Mechanics grew package solution sales 37 percent year on year to KRW 771.6 billion in the second quarter of 2026, on substrates for AI accelerators, server processors and cars11.
   + AT&S, an Austrian firm, calls itself a globally leading maker of high-end package substrates38. It makes them in China and Malaysia, and since its 2025/26 financial year also in Austria39. Citing the research firm Prismark, its annual report for 2025/26 valued the market for flip-chip ball grid array (FC-BGA) substrates at $7.5 billion in 2025, up 18 percent from 20247.
   + Source Unimicron 2026 Q2 earnings conference 10
   + TTM Technologies says it regularly builds printed circuit boards of more than 30 layers and can go past 705. Those layers carry power to the GPUs and route fast signals between them, so how much signal the laminate absorbs is a main design constraint. Nitto Boseki also makes NE-glass, a low-dielectric glass cloth that absorbs less signal, used in switches and routers in data centers45.
   + Data center computing was 24 percent of TTM's fiscal 2025 revenue of about $2.9 billion, against 14 percent two years earlier5.
   + Board makers are adding plants outside China and Taiwan, where TTM says most of its circuit board competitors are based5. In its annual report for 2025, TTM said it was building a plant in Syracuse, New York, to make ultra-high-density circuit boards for national security needs5. It expected volume production there in the second half of 20265. Nikkei Asia reported in September 2025 that Thailand aimed to become the biggest circuit board hub outside China as board makers moved to diversify production46.
   + The few firms that build substrates are spending to add capacity, but it takes years. In August 2026 Ibiden said that, with supply short across the industry, the average price of package substrates had risen more than it expected4. The materials inside the substrate are harder to replace than the firms that build it. Mitsubishi Gas Chemical, a laminate maker, says chipmakers and electronic device makers further down the chain approve the materials it sells to substrate makers6. So a rival to Ajinomoto's film or to Nitto Boseki's low-expansion glass cloth would first need approval from those chipmakers and device makers.

   words only after:  Chokepoint card
   - High
   - Rated on test tools, where Advantest held 62.7 percent of the market in 2025 and Teradyne 21.8 percent, by CSET's analysis of TechInsights data1.
   - market for chip testers in 2025, by Advantest's estimate2
   - About 65% of the tester market in 2025, by its own estimate2
   - $2.52bn of semiconductor test revenue in 2025, service included3
   - Ran 8,348 testers in Q2 2026, up from 6,797 a year earlier4
   - $6.71bn of 2025 revenue5
   - 87% of the $1.9bn market for dicing saws and grinders in 2025, on CSET's analysis of TechInsights data6
   - Customers such as ASE already buy testers from both firms7. The barrier is cost, because switching means rebuilding a customer's whole test setup, Advantest says8.
   - Georgetown's CSET, analyzing TechInsights data, finds that between 2019 and 2024 Chinese toolmakers gained 5 percentage points of world market share in test tools and 4 points in fabrication tools built for advanced packaging9. But they lost share in bonding and other conventional assembly and packaging tools9.
   - American firms held 23.5 percent of the market for test tools in 2025 and Japanese firms 63.0 percent, by CSET's analysis of TechInsights data1. Amkor raised its planned investment in an Arizona packaging and test campus to $7 billion in October 202510. In June 2026 it announced a long-term partnership with TSMC, which will buy packaging and test services from it in Arizona11.
   - In a TrendForce ranking cited by CSET, four of the ten largest chip assembly and test contractors by 2024 revenue are headquartered in China9. Georgetown's CSET, analyzing TechInsights data, finds that between 2019 and 2024 Chinese toolmakers gained 5 percentage points of world market share in test tools9. Across assembly, test and packaging tools, they still lag significantly behind foreign suppliers in market share9. Advantest says that switching to another tester maker means rebuilding a customer's whole test setup, at significant extra cost8.
   - Turning silicon into a finished, packaged chip takes 1,000 to 1,500 steps, depending on how complex the chip is, the Semiconductor Industry Association estimated in 202512. Of all those steps, only test puts electrical signals through the chip, Advantest's investor guide of July 2026 says13. The earlier stages of chipmaking make dies, the individual chips on a wafer. The back end of the industry, assembly and test, checks which of them work and puts the good ones into packages.
   - Source Advantest FY2025 results briefing, April 2026 2
   - Source Advantest FY2025 results briefing, April 2026 2
   - Advantest puts its own share at about 65 percent of an estimated $9.0 billion tester market in 20252. Its share of system-on-chip (SoC) testers, the machines that test processors and other non-memory chips, rose about ten points in a year to 66 percent2. It held about 60 percent of memory test2.
   - Teradyne, Advantest's main rival, took $2.52 billion of semiconductor test revenue in 2025, service included3. Of that, $1.89 billion came from SoC testers and $505 million from memory testers3. Teradyne's total revenue grew 13 percent to $3.19 billion in 2025, with fourth-quarter revenue up 44 percent on AI demand19.
   - Georgetown University's Center for Security and Emerging Technology (CSET) makes a second estimate, from data by the research firm TechInsights1. It gives Advantest 62.7 percent of a $9.1 billion market for test tools in 2025 and Teradyne 21.8 percent1. Japanese firms held 63.0 percent of that market and American firms 23.5 percent1.
   - Dicing and grinding. Dicing saws cut wafers into dies, and grinders thin them. CSET's analysis of TechInsights data found that in 2025 Disco held 87 percent and Accretech 8.1 percent of the $1.9 billion market for these machines6. Panel-level packaging builds packages on large panels in place of 300 mm wafers, to make more of them at once25. In December 2025 Disco announced a saw that cuts such panels, as large as 400 by 400 mm, into individual packages25.
   - Taiwanese firms lead outsourced assembly and test, the OSAT business, and Chinese firms are large challengers. Taiwan's ASE billed $20,573.4 million in 20257. Packaging made up about 48 percent of that, testing about 11 percent and its electronics manufacturing arm about 40 percent31. In a TrendForce ranking that CSET cites, four of the top ten OSATs by 2024 revenue are headquartered in China9.
   - Taiwan's assembly and test contractors are expanding. ASE ran 6,797 testers in the second quarter of 2025 and 8,348 a year later4. It spent $2.7 billion on machinery and $1.4 billion on buildings, facilities and automation in the first half of 202635. In July 2026 it said revenue from leading-edge advanced packaging was running ahead of its $3.5 billion forecast for 202635. Powertech, another Taiwanese assembly and test firm, aims to run the first panel-level packaging line for AI chips in 202736.
   - Amkor, an American assembly and test contractor, took $6.71 billion of revenue in 20255. Its revenue in the second quarter of 2026 alone was $1.90 billion, up 26 percent year on year37. By October 2025 it had raised the planned investment in its Arizona campus to $7 billion10.
   - In October 2024 TSMC and Amkor signed a memorandum of understanding for TSMC to buy packaging and test services from Amkor's planned plant in Peoria, Arizona38. TSMC would use those services for its customers, especially those using its Phoenix fabs38. The two announced a long-term partnership in June 202611.
   - Source ASE Technology Holding, second quarter 2026 earnings release (SEC Form 6-K) 4
   - Customers can switch tester makers, but at a cost. Advantest says that switching to another tester maker means rebuilding the whole test setup used for development, evaluation and mass production, at significant extra cost to the customer8. Still, ASE says it buys testers with similar functions from different makers, including Teradyne and Advantest7.
   - Chinese toolmakers are gaining share in test and advanced packaging tools, but CSET finds they still lag significantly behind foreign suppliers in market share9. Between 2019 and 2024 they gained 5 percentage points of world market share in test tools and 4 points in fabrication tools built for advanced packaging, by CSET's analysis of TechInsights data9. But they lost share in bonding and other conventional assembly and packaging tools9.
   - Assembly and test contractors such as ASE and Amkor earn a small part of what the chip industry sells. ASE's packaging business took $9,829.2 million from outside customers in 2025, and its testing business $2,292.0 million7. Amkor's revenue was $6.71 billion5. Global semiconductor sales that year were $791.7 billion39.
